# MeetingPipe backlog index

Generated by `python3 scripts/backlog.py index` from the header of every [`tasks/<ID>.md`](./tasks/). Do not edit it by hand: change the task's header and regenerate. CI fails when this file is stale. Process rules, priority bands and band origins live in [`meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md`](./meetingpipe-q6-backlog.md); the owner-verification gate lives in [`owner-verification.md`](./owner-verification.md).

## Active (P1 and P2): 284

| [DET13](./tasks/DET13.md) | The reprompt cooldown drops the live candidate, so a back-to-back meeting gets no prompt and a manual Record arms no end detection | Detection | partial | P1 | Filed 2026-08-10 from the owner's 2026-08-04 notes; END9 already named this cooldown under "Observed but not filed here". They ended a Teams call at 12:32:41, joined another 22 s later, got **no prompt at all** (no `lifecycle starting`, no `prompt_shown`), pressed Record by hand at 12:33:03, and the recording then **never stopped when that call ended** and ran 15 m 35 s until hand-stopped. Both halves are in the code: `handleDiscovery`'s two suppression branches are asymmetric, where the skip-latch branch calls `noteSuppressedCandidate(source)` (DET7, whose comment predicts this exact failure) and the `isCoolingDown` branch beside it at `MeetingSessionController.swift:1140` is a bare `return`, so for the 60 s `repromptCooldownSec` window the same bundle yields neither a prompt nor a retained candidate and `startManual` falls to `beginRecording(source: nil)`. Confirmed on disk: `20260804-123303.meta.json` has `source_bundle_id: null` while the detected meetings either side carry `com.microsoft.teams2`, so no workflow, no source chip, and no AX end anchor. The window is directly observable twice, as re-prompts at exactly 65 s after the preceding end on 2026-08-04 and 2026-08-06. **P1 because a real meeting was captured headless and only stopped because the owner noticed; the owner may re-band, since no audio was lost.** Scope: make a cooldown-suppressed sighting attachable (note that `liveSuppressedCandidate` gates on `isSkipLatched`, so the write alone is not enough), then separately decide whether a genuinely different call in the same app should be prompted. Explicitly not shortening the cooldown, not auto-resuming without consent (END9's title veto reasoning stands), and not the mic-busy oracle the owner floated, which DET10 shows has produced zero spans on this Mac. **Partial 2026-08-18: change 1 shipped, change 2 evaluated and not built.** `handleDiscovery`'s `isCoolingDown` branch keeps its sighting now, and `liveSuppressedCandidate()` serves both suppressions: a skip-latched candidate keeps DET7's latch gate unchanged, a cooling-down one falls through to the sighting's age against a new `suppressedCandidateGraceSec`, defined as the skip latch's grace so it stays derived from the live `DiscoveryScanCadence` rather than hardcoded (DET9's rule) and cannot drift from the blackout both bridge. So a manual Record inside the 60 s window now produces an attached recording: workflow rules see the app, the sidecar carries its identity, and `armLifecycleLeaveButton` has something to arm on. Retaining a sighting arms no skip latch, so the call still prompts once the window lapses, and each retention logs `coordinator`/`cooldown_candidate_retained` once (not once per poll), which the 2026-08-04 occurrence had no equivalent of. 7 new tests, 5 of them red against `main` first and 2 fences green throughout by design; `swift build` clean, `swift test` green, all three truth fences clean, and the Python half run to prove it untouched. `CONVENTIONS.md` documents the event, `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s **Suppressed candidate** entry was rewritten (its "lifetime *is* the latch's" claim stopped being true), and `README.md` plus `docs/GUIDE.md` say the menu row now covers a back-to-back meeting, not only a skipped one. **What change 2 owes, and the finding:** neither discriminator survives this corpus. The window title is nil at this seam by construction, since all three `MeetingSourceScanner` sites omit it and `MeetingTitleResolver` runs only at prompt time behind the `engageLifecycle` this branch returns before, so a title veto degrades to prompting on 100% of sightings and is a no-op; separately, the Scope bullet's two "titles differ" pairs are END9's LLM-written summary titles, a different field from CAL3's 3%-covered AX window title. The confident-winner gap has overlapping classes: false-end signature returns measured at +0.24 s to +48.2 s, while this task's own genuine back-to-back call was sighted at +22 s, so any threshold above 22 s suppresses the very meeting DET13 exists to rescue, and the only two genuine points above it (+67.6 / +64.6) are the cluster END9 warns is floored by the cooldown itself. The spec's fallback (change 1 plus a visible affordance) is therefore live, and the affordance was **deliberately not built**: a new surface needs a rendered eyeball a menu-bar app cannot give a session, so it is a decision for the owner rather than code. **Closed 2026-08-19: the owner chose nothing further.** Change 1 already makes the menu bar's **Record this app meeting** row appear for a back-to-back rather than only for a skipped one, which is a proportionate affordance for a 60 second window. Change 2 is refuted rather than merely unbuilt (title nil on 100% of sightings at this seam; the +22 s incident sits inside the gap band), so reviving it is a fresh spec, not a pickup of this row. **DET13's dev half is done.** **Field evidence 2026-08-19:** the scenario recurred in the owner's real use (two Teams calls 4.2 s apart) and change 1 held. Walkthrough steps 2, 3 and 5 are answered from events.jsonl and the sidecar: `cooldown_candidate_retained` fired (an event that did not exist before this task), `record_attached` carried the Teams identity, `20260819-104026.meta.json` reads source_bundle_id com.microsoft.teams2 where 2026-08-04 read null, and zero re-prompts followed the stop. **Step 4 was NOT exercised**: the second recording was hand-stopped (a bare recording-to-stopping state change with no lifecycle/ended before it), so it cannot show the attached recording would have ended by itself. **Remainder: walkthrough step 4, then the close word.** Full evidence with line references in the Progress section of [`tasks/DET13.md`](./tasks/DET13.md). Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band (D098): seed the candidate in abandonPrompt. **Amendment shipped 2026-11-16**: abandonPrompt seeds the prompt's own source (DET18's offerPrompt already did for a timeout), so a hand start seconds after Skip, the close or a force stop attaches with the lifecycle engaged; an end-withdrawn prompt still seeds nothing. 5 new tests (Skip and force-stop red before the fix); ci-local green. **Remainder: walkthrough step 4, the amendment's field bar on the next pack, then the close word.** |

| [END9](./tasks/END9.md) | One empty AX walk 120 ms after another stops a live recording, and the new Teams calling UI made it frequent | Detection | partial | P1 | Filed 2026-08-04. Three false ends on 2026-08-03 chopped live Teams calls; the 12:51:19Z one lost 9 m 53 s of meeting (the call really ended 13:01:13Z) and its re-prompt then timed out to skip. `rescueProvisionalEnd` treats one empty re-walk 120 ms after the signal's own as proof, and `confirmProvisionalEnd` (`PromotionEngine.swift:221`) bypasses both the debounce and the `requiresCorroboration` guard built for this signal; the Teams accessibility tree goes empty for 1-65 s at a time, so both walks land in one blackout. 6 verified false ends, 4 of them in the six days from 2026-07-29, matching the owner's switch to the new Teams calling UI (an amplifier, not the cause: 2026-06-09 predates it). The owner's collapse/mini-panel hypothesis is **refuted by direct measurement** and the spec records how, so it is not re-derived. Fix: gate the confirm on Teams' own SlimCore `UnregisterCall` teardown line (45/53 confirm, 8 hold, every verified false end held). Owner-requested execution order: double-verify the root cause, write the failing test first, fix last. P1 as silent meeting loss; owner may re-band. Lands after DET9, which edits the same `handleDiscovery` lines. **Amended 2026-08-10 with three more instances, the first since filing, both reported by the owner as unexplained:** `20260804-133028` stopped after **21 s**, `20260804-133158` after **30 s** (a rescue at +15 s then a confirm 15 s later, so one meeting chopped twice in two minutes across two stems, after which the owner gave up for the afternoon), and `20260806-163013` after **1 m 46 s** (recovered only via a timed-out reprompt then an accepted one, leaving a fragment plus a 22.7 min body with a 2.8 min hole). All three carry the exact signature and all three fall inside the new-calling-UI window the pre-registered A/B tests, so the fix is unchanged. The amendment also corrects UX45's reading of `20260804-133028`: its "no-speech skip path working as designed" is true but incomplete, since it had no speech because this defect cut it 20 s in.  **Built 2026-08-20 after instance 18**, the owner's `20260820-123036` cut at 8.5 s while they were still in the call: `shareable_content_window_present` and `process_audio_is_running_input` were both still true and unchanged since detection, only the Leave button moved, and Teams logged no `UnregisterCall` within 27 minutes either side. Execution-contract step 3 was re-run first: 58 confirm / 18 hold against the filed 45/8, a confirm-rate drop of 84.9% to 76.3% that is **not** oracle drift, settled by splitting on leading signal rather than reading the aggregate (`process_audio_is_running_input_false` is 4 for 4 with zero holds, and all four of that day's real ends confirm at 0.22-0.81 s). The rate fell because ten more recordings had been chopped. **Shipped:** the pure `CallTeardownOracle`, `SlimCoreTeardownLog` (line predicate only, reusing `SlimCoreMuteLog`'s resolver, tail and UTC parse), the session-lived `CallTeardownWatcher`, the gate in `rescueProvisionalEnd`'s empty-walk branch, trim-on-confirm via `RecordingPostProcessor.applyTrim` capping REC9's target rather than adding a second `-t`, and the HUD `Confirming end` state. Degrades to today's behaviour when the log is unreadable, so it can only be neutral or better; the 150 s cap is itself a confirm and its event rows are the drift detector. +28 tests; neutering `decide` to an unconditional confirm (exactly what `main` does) turns 9 red, restored and re-run green. `swift test` 2868 0 failures, release build clean, fences green. **Still open, hence `partial`:** the ~5 s `endDebounceFloor` for the sub-2 s flicker class the oracle structurally cannot reach, the empty-vs-failed AX walk result type, and the title veto (which only binds once an auto-resume exists). **Owner-owed:** walkthrough steps 2 and 4, proposed weight `simple` (use it through a normal day of Teams calls and confirm no recording is cut mid-meeting; eyeball the HUD's `Confirming end` state if a hold ever opens). Needs the owner's approval before the row moves to `owner-verification.md`. Note walkthrough step 2's unified-log census is unrunnable at anything but immediate notice: retention had already rolled off instance 18 by the time it was investigated. |
| [END10](./tasks/END10.md) | END9's teardown oracle is never started, so every provisional end still confirms on the walk that found nothing | Detection | partial | P1 | Filed 2026-08-21 from the owner reporting that recordings "often drop at the beginning for no obvious reason". [END9](./tasks/END9.md) shipped 2026-08-20 to stop exactly this and does not run: `Coordinator` holds `callTeardown` with a doc comment saying it is "started once at session setup", and `callTeardown.start()` appears nowhere under `daemon/Sources` (the only call is in `CallTeardownHoldTests`). `State.available` initialises `false` and its only writer is inside `tick()`, which is reachable only from `start()`, so `decide`'s opening `guard input.oracleAvailable else { return .confirm(reason: .oracleUnavailable) }` fires on every consult and `beginTeardownHold` collapses into `confirmProvisionalEnd()` before it can create its timer, emit `lifecycle_end_held`, or show the HUD. A second defect would keep it broken after starting: `rearm` runs inside the `readQueue.async` closure and `defaultScheduler` uses `Timer.scheduledTimer`, which attaches to `RunLoop.current`, so on a GCD worker with no run loop the tail ticks exactly once; MIC16's `LogMuteAdapter`, whose threading END9 says it mirrors, hops to main first and END9's copy dropped the hop. **Field proof:** the only two END9-instrumented rows in existence are both false positives, 2026-08-21T10:36:48.985Z with `held_sec: 7.03e-06` chopping `20260821-133615.wav` at 31.6 s while the call ran to 13:56, and 2026-08-21 16:31:38 local with `held_sec: 0.0106` chopping `20260821-163018.wav` at 78.7 s, both carrying `oracle: "oracle_unavailable"` and `trimmed: false`; `lifecycle_end_held` has zero occurrences across every log generation. **Cost 2026-07-13 to 2026-08-21:** 16 meetings chopped then restarted by hand within 10 minutes, 14 short fragments published into the vault as their own notes, 10 more producing no note at all; the 2026-08-17 chop is provably false, since discovery still reported `leave_button: true` on the same pid 33 s after the end was confirmed. **P1 because the broken promise is that a recording lasts as long as the meeting**; the owner may re-band. Scope: start the watcher, re-arm on a live run loop, then make the oracle trustworthy once it runs (hysteresis on `available`, a logged error instead of the `try?` in `SlimCoreMuteLog.generations` that hides why the shared resolver reports the family missing 292 times across 45 sessions, a freshness check since the generation froze at 13:36:15 while the call ran to 13:56, `lastTeardownAt` scoped to the recording, and name-ordered generations), plus a fence on the wiring rather than the unit. Folded deliberately: starting the watcher without the trust items is a fix that appears to work and then chops at a lower rate. Explicitly not the `endDebounceFloor`, not the empty-vs-failed AX walk type, not `confirmProvisionalEnd`'s bypass (verified deliberate 2026-08-21), and not the manual/attach gap, which is END11. Spec in [`tasks/END10.md`](./tasks/END10.md). **Shipped 2026-08-21.** All 8 scope items landed. The watcher is started from `Coordinator.start()` and the tail re-arms on a `DispatchSourceTimer`, since a run-loop `Timer` scheduled from `readQueue` fires exactly once; `available` now needs 3 consecutive failed resolves to drop (measured: all 323 `log_mute_source_missing` rows from 2026-08-05 to 2026-08-21 are isolated single ticks, longest run 1, zero runs of 2+); the resolver returns a `Listing` carrying the `NSError` domain and code instead of a `try?`; a generation not written since the recording began confirms as the new `log_stale` reason rather than holding the cap; the teardown lookback can no longer reach back past the recording's own start; and generations rank by the name's stamp with mtime as fallback. Fenced by the new `CoordinatorWiringFenceTests` (brace-matches the body of `Coordinator.start()`) and `CallTeardownWatcherTests`, which did not exist before and is why END9 shipped dead. 6 of the 7 behavioural tests were measured red against the unfixed code; item 4's cannot be, since the API it asserts on did not exist. swift build + release clean (no new warnings), 2891 swift tests green, 1344 pytest green, truth fences green. **Remainder is owner-only:** the spec's field bar, one working day of Teams calls then two greps, **re-stated by END12 on 2026-09-02**: the `lifecycle_end_held` count proves nothing (8 of the 9 holds in the field opened on ends corroboration had already confirmed, and only 1 ever released), and item 5's `log_stale` rule was refuted in the field and removed there, so the bar to read is END12's three greps, not this row's two. Needs owner approval to move to owner-verification.md (proposed weight complex: it needs real calls and dwell time). |
| [END11](./tasks/END11.md) | An attached or manual recording has no end detection, because armLifecycleLeaveButton arms an adapter that was never engaged | Detection | partial | P1 | Filed 2026-08-21 from the owner reporting that "after starting manually connected to Teams it is not dropping when the meeting obviously ended". `MeetingLifecycleCoordinator.armLeaveButton(_:)` is `activeAdapter?.armLeaveButton(element)`, whose doc comment already says it no-ops without a live adapter; `activeAdapter` is set only by `engage(context:handle:)`, whose only caller is `engageLifecycle(for:)`, whose only caller is `handleDiscovery`, and both of that function's suppression branches `return` before it. So every explicit start resolves the Leave button correctly and then drops it on the floor: no adapter, no `lifecycle`/`in_meeting`, no Leave signal, no end. **This is the half [DET13](./tasks/DET13.md) believed change 1 had closed**: its row records "`armLifecycleLeaveButton` has something to arm on", and DET13 did make the *element* reachable by retaining the cooldown-suppressed sighting, but not the *adapter*, since the retaining branch is the branch that returns before `engageLifecycle`. Not a regression and not a reopen, the neighbouring half was assumed to follow. **Field proof 2026-08-21, twice.** The 16:32:04 instance is the decisive one, since the attach landed 26 s after a false end and therefore inside `repromptCooldownSec`, the exact window DET13 change 1 was built for: `record_attached {trigger: "manual", bundle_id: "com.microsoft.teams2"}`, `ax_handles_built` with `found_leave: true`, the whole mute side armed, and no `lifecycle`/`in_meeting`, no `ax_leave_button_self_armed`, no `ax_leave_button_state`. Earlier the same day: `record_attached {trigger: "manual", bundle_id: "com.microsoft.teams2"}` at 13:38:00 is followed by `ax_handles_built` (`found_leave: true`) and `mute_poller_started` and by no `lifecycle`/`in_meeting`, no `ax_leave_button_self_armed` and no `ax_leave_button_state` for the whole 18 m 30 s, ending in a bare `state_change` to `stopping` with no `lifecycle`/`ended`; the auto path 90 s earlier emits all three within 900 ms. The pure `bundle_id: "manual"` path is worse: `20260814-163455.wav` and `20260817-164312.wav` have no `recording_stopped` row at all, and the second ran **72 minutes** holding the idle-sleep assertion, through `system_capture_lost`, a full system sleep and a 106.8 s wake gap, having been started 11 seconds after a false end chopped the meeting it belonged to. **P1 because the broken promise is that a recording ends when the meeting does**, and the failure is unbounded rather than a late stop; the owner may re-band, since no audio is lost. Scope: engage the lifecycle on an explicit start that carries a source (preferring the shared seam DET7 lifted so the four entry points cannot drift), decide and pin a bound for the source-less case that has none today, stop leaking the sleep assertion, and guard `handleMeetingEnded` on bundle identity. Explicitly not changing whether an end is honoured (that branch's "a missed real end is worse" comment is sound and is the opposite failure), not stopping the cooldown arming on a false end (DET13 records the cooldown as load-bearing against re-prompt storms, and END10 removes the false ends instead), not the cooldown's visibility (DET13 already ships `cooldown_candidate_retained`), and not reworking `IdleStopBackstop`. Spec in [`tasks/END11.md`](./tasks/END11.md). **Shipped 2026-08-21.** All 4 scope items landed. `beginRecording` now engages the lifecycle for any explicit start carrying a source, placed there rather than in `recordAttached` so the menu row, the hotkey, the URL verb and the start-late notification cannot drift; guarded on the new `MeetingLifecycleCoordinator.isEngaged`, which is load-bearing because `engage()` opens with `disengage()` and would otherwise reset a live discovery-engaged session. `armLeaveButton` against a nil adapter now emits `lifecycle`/`arm_leave_button_dropped` instead of being a silent no-op. `handleMeetingEnded` ignores an end naming a different app than the recording's own source, and deliberately still honours one for a source-less recording, which has no identity to compare and no AX anchor either. Item 2 decided: a source-less recording engages nothing and is bounded only by the mic-only silence backstop, which is already fed on every start; now stated in `docs/GUIDE.md`. A duration ceiling was considered and rejected, since adding a second chopper to this band trades a rare visible failure for a common invisible one. **Item 3 was partly mis-specified:** both terminal paths already released the sleep assertion and the 111/110 imbalance traces to a process that died with no `app_terminate`, but a real leak sat next door, `stop()`'s opening guard returning before the release, and that is fixed. 3 of the tests measured red against the unfixed code; the engage itself is pinned by a source fence (ordered before the arm) because `engageLifecycle` needs a real pid no unit test can supply. swift build + release clean (no new warnings), 2902 swift tests green, truth fences green. **Remainder is owner-only:** the field bar, one hand-started recording on a live Teams call. Needs owner approval to move to owner-verification.md (proposed weight complex: needs a real call). Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: a source-less recording ends only on the 900 s backstop and keeps the whole silent tail (315 s on 20261021-103054, 44 minutes on 20260923-154340); add a short confirm after the silence notice, trim the tail, report silent_tail_sec. |
| [END12](./tasks/END12.md) | END10's stale-log rule confirms every mid-call provisional end, because Teams' SlimCore log is buffered and event-driven and its mtime never moves during a live call | Detection | partial | P1 | Filed 2026-09-02 from the owner reporting that a detected recording stops when they move across other windows, frequent on 2026-09-01 and once on 2026-09-02. [END10](./tasks/END10.md) shipped 2026-08-21 and is the installed build; its item 5 makes `CallTeardownOracle.decide` confirm as `log_stale` whenever the SlimCore generation's mtime predates the recording start, on the synchronous first consult, before the timer, `lifecycle_end_held`, or the HUD exist, so the hold END9 built collapses into the pre-END9 chop under a new reason string. The premise is false for this log: it is event-driven (a steady call writes nothing, and the join burst lands 2 to 4 s before `recording_started`) and buffered (the mute reader on the same file measured lines reaching disk 0.2 s to 17 m 59 s after their own stamp, the longest flushed by the call's own `UnregisterCall`). **Field cost since 2026-08-22:** 12 oracle confirms, 11 `log_stale` with `held_sec` in microseconds, 1 `teardown_observed`; 8 of the 11 were Leave-button-led and every one was followed by a re-prompt or a manual re-attach within 4 to 84 s; on 2026-09-01 the cuts at 13:17 and 16:50 preceded Teams' own teardowns at 13:28 and 16:55 and both re-prompts timed out. END10's field bar (`lifecycle_end_held` present, no `oracle_unavailable`) passes throughout, because 8 of its 9 holds opened on ends corroboration had already confirmed. **P1 because the broken promise is END10's own, that a recording lasts as long as the meeting**; the owner may re-band. Scope: drop the instant confirm on staleness so a stale-looking generation holds to the teardown or the 150 s cap (`hold_cap_reached` stays the drift detector); if a dead-log bound is kept, measure it against the family's hourly heartbeat, never the recording start; flip the two oracle tests that pin the rule and add the 2026-09-02 12:36 and 10:10 replays on the host side; re-state END10's field bar against the promise. Explicitly not the AX invalidation, the debounce floor, the cooldown, or the first-consult ordering. Adjacent and unfiled: the 8,424-row `call_teardown_source_missing` storm (never three in a row, so it cut nothing) and the same flush delay's effect on MIC8's log mute readings. Spec in [`tasks/END12.md`](./tasks/END12.md). **Shipped 2026-09-02.** Item 1 landed: `CallTeardownOracle.decide` no longer reads the generation's mtime and `Reason.logStale` is gone, so a quiet log holds to the teardown or the 150 s cap and only an unreadable one still confirms; item 2 deliberately not taken (no dead-log bound at all), item 3 kept as specified, and `lifecycle_end_held` now carries `log_written_sec_ago` so a cadence bound could be measured from the field rather than guessed. Tests: two oracle tests flipped (`testGenerationNotWrittenSinceTheRecordingBeganStillHolds`, `testATeardownInsideTheLookbackConfirmsHoweverStaleTheMtimeLooks`) and two host replays added to `CallTeardownHoldTests` (`test_a_generation_not_written_since_the_recording_began_still_holds`, the 12:36 shape; `test_a_teardown_landing_inside_the_hold_confirms_despite_a_stale_looking_mtime`, the 10:10 shape), all four measured red against the unfixed oracle (7 assertion failures across the 4) before the change. Measured on this base: `swift build` and `swift build -c release` clean with zero warnings, `swift test` 2914 executed, 1 skipped, 0 failures (4 new), truth fences green. Docs: `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` rewrote the frozen-generation entry with the flush measurement and qualified the degrade-toward-old-behaviour rule; `daemon/CLAUDE.md` MIC8 line gained the flush-clock clause; END10's remainder re-stated above; README, ARCHITECTURE, CONVENTIONS and GUIDE checked and untouched (no oracle reason or HUD copy lives there). One more `log_stale` row landed after filing, 2026-09-02 13:05:56 on a process-audio-led real end, making it 12 of 13 consults. **Not installed:** landing on `main` is not `./scripts/rebuild.sh`, and the field bar cannot start until the owner installs. **Remainder is owner-only:** the field bar, a working day of Teams calls with window switching then the three greps under Acceptance; needs owner approval to move to owner-verification.md (proposed weight complex: real calls and dwell time). |
| [END13](./tasks/END13.md) | The hold's 150 s cap confirms an end that a live signal is actively contradicting, and the fast restart it provokes attaches to nothing | Detection | partial | P1 | Filed 2026-09-04 from the owner checking their last meeting and finding it had stopped itself mid-call. [END12](./tasks/END12.md) works (7 holds since its 2026-09-02 17:16 build, zero `log_stale`, against 11 in the 12 days before) and exposed the next layer: `CallTeardownOracle.decide` ends on a bare wall clock, `guard elapsed < input.holdCapSec else { return .confirm(reason: .holdCapReached) }`, which reads no signal but the teardown line. **On 2026-09-04 it cut `20260904-133358.wav` at 160.8 s of hold while four independent readings said the call was live**: `ProcessAudioSignal` had reported `value: true` at 13:33:52 and never reported an end (it emits only on change, and `process_audio_object_resolved` still listed 5 objects at 14:02); the mute poller re-resolved `buttons_found: 1` at 14:05:16 and logged `ax_mute_button_state_kept` once a second for the rest of the hold; Teams has logged no `UnregisterCall` for that call to this day; and the owner pressed Record again 4.2 s later and stayed in the meeting another 40 minutes. The tree blanked as END9 measured (`mute_state_cleared_blind` `blind_polls: 3` at 14:05:14, alongside the Leave invalidation at 14:05:10) and then came back **without a Leave button**: `ax_mute_button_rearmed` at 14:05:19, `ax_leave_button_rearmed` never, against 5.4 s on the 2026-09-02 hold that released. Best supported reading is the compact call view, whose Calling-controls container has no Leave button, so the cap is timing a rendering state. Cost: the hold's audio is trimmed on confirm, so 2 m 49 s of meeting has no recording at all and the meeting is split across two library rows. Second half: the replacement carries `bundle_id: "manual"`, so per [END11](./tasks/END11.md) it has no end anchor and cannot stop itself. `startManual` attaches only when `DetectionStateMachine.liveSuppressedCandidate()` is populated, and that slot is written only by a discovery poll's cooldown branch, which cannot run until after the stop; measured across the corpus the first retention lands 1.1 s to 14.1 s after a stop, and the owner beat it at 4.2 s. **P1 because the broken promise is the same one END12 restored, that a recording lasts as long as the meeting**; the owner may re-band. Scope: a live cross-class signal vetoes the cap (tri-state, so an unresolved probe still gets today's bound, which is the DET8 failure mode); an absolute ceiling above the veto with its own reason string; gate the "the teardown line has moved" WARN on the no-evidence case, since it fired on a live call whose line was merely unflushed; and write the just-stopped source into the candidate slot at `stopRecording` so a fast manual restart attaches without waiting for a poll. Explicitly not: nudging `defaultHoldCapSec`, `requiresCorroboration` or the `confirmProvisionalEnd` bypass (both re-verified deliberate), making process audio a promoting signal, the cooldown, DET14's clear (the slot was empty, so there was nothing to clear), END11's source-less bound, or the accessibility trigger and the re-arm asymmetry. Adjacent and unfiled: `IdleStopBackstop` has fired zero times in the entire event log and is the only bound a source-less recording has; and `call_teardown_source_missing` fired twice inside the hold, still never three in a row and still unexplained. Spec in [`tasks/END13.md`](./tasks/END13.md). **Shipped 2026-09-04.** All five items. `CallTeardownOracle.Input` gained `liveCallSignal` (`LiveCallSignal`, tri-state: only `.callIsUp` vetoes; `.unknown` and `.callIsDown` keep today's 150 s bound, which is the DET8 shape handled rather than re-created) and `liveSignalCeilingSec`; `decide` holds past the cap while the veto stands and confirms at the ceiling under a new `live_signal_ceiling_reached` reason, with nothing above the cap touched so a teardown still confirms on the first consult. **The ceiling is 7200 s and the number is a measurement**: across 269 paired recordings in the whole event log the longest ran 109.7 min, p99 96.7, p95 58.2, so no meeting the owner has held reaches it, and the asymmetry is the argument (an early ceiling re-creates this chop; a late one costs only latency, since the hold's audio is trimmed back to the provisional either way). The reading is process audio only, cached on `MeetingLifecycleCoordinator` from its own signal events under the existing generation guard and read through a new `SessionHost.liveCallSignal`; `shareableContentWindow` shares the ax-leave lead's evidence class and is deliberately excluded. The drift WARN moved into `MeetingSessionController.teardownDriftWarning(for:)` and fires on `hold_cap_reached` only, so the 2026-09-04 line claiming Teams' teardown line had moved cannot recur on a live call; `lifecycle_end_hold_cap_vetoed` is emitted once per hold when the veto engages, and `lifecycle_end_held` / `lifecycle_provisional_end_confirmed` now carry `live_signal`. Item 5: `stopRecording` writes the just-stopped source into the suppressed-candidate slot synchronously with `.cooldown` origin, so a Record pressed straight after a stop attaches without waiting for a discovery poll. **That softens DET14 by 24 s and it is stated rather than left to be discovered**: a genuinely finished call can show the menu row for that long where DET14 left 0, against the two minutes DET14 measured, and 24 s is already the width derived from the one quiet poll this write bridges. Tests: 5 in `CallTeardownOracleTests`, 4 in `CallTeardownHoldTests`, 3 in `MeetingLifecycleCoordinatorTests`, 2 in `MeetingSessionControllerBranchTests`; the two 2026-09-04 replays and the ceiling test measured red against the unfixed oracle first (11 assertion failures across 5 tests), and the unresolved-probe test passes both ways by design, since it pins the path that must not change. Measured on this base: `swift build` and `swift build -c release` clean with no new warnings (two pre-existing Swift 6 warnings in untouched files), `swift test` 2929 executed, 1 skipped, 0 failures, truth fences green. Docs: `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` gained "A wall clock is not evidence either"; one rule line in `daemon/CLAUDE.md`; `ARCHITECTURE.md` updated in three places, one of which also cleared an END12 leftover still describing the deleted `log_stale` rule; `README.md` and `docs/GUIDE.md` gained the fast-restart clause; `CONVENTIONS.md` and `design/` checked and untouched (no new event category, no UI surface changed). **HUD unchanged deliberately:** a vetoed hold shows `Confirming end` for its whole length, which can now be hours, kept on END9's own reasoning that a hold nothing surfaces is a bug report rather than an observation; if it reads as alarming the fix is a distinct state, not silence. **Not installed:** the binary at `~/Applications/MeetingPipe.app` is still END12's 2026-09-02 17:16 build, so nothing can be confirmed in the field until the owner runs `./scripts/rebuild.sh`. **Remainder is owner-only:** the walkthrough in `tasks/END13.md`, one live Teams call with four to five minutes spent in other windows, then leaving it, then a grep of that day's `events.jsonl`; needs owner approval to move to owner-verification.md (proposed weight complex: a real call and dwell time). |

| [REC17](./tasks/REC17.md) | A system-audio start that never returns is invisible to every fence, so 64 minutes of the far side went missing with no banner and the idle backstop nudged as if everyone had left | Recording | partial | P1 | Filed 2026-09-17 from the owner asking why the "Still in a meeting?" notification fired twice during a live Teams call. `20260917-143029.wav`: `recorder started` 11:30:29Z, `SCStream started` 12:34:41Z, **64 min 12 s later**; `system.wav` sat at its 4 KB header the whole time, the merge padded a `system_delay=3852.29s` head of silence, and the stop posted no warning because `system_audio_frames` was 84777 and `systemDegraded` false. Nothing bounds `SystemAudioCapture.start()`, `checkSystemLiveness` engages only after the first buffer, `retrySystemAudio` no-ops on the non-nil hung capture, and the idle backstop reads a channel that never came up as silence, so muting at 11:53:49Z started its streak: `silence_notified` 11:57:49Z, `auto_stop_silence_skipped` 12:01:49Z re-nudging. **The broken promise is REC4's: a missing far side is surfaced, never silent**; owner may re-band. Scope: bound the start with `runWithTimeout` and fence the late completion, route the timeout through `handleSystemAudioLost`, and hold the backstop while the system channel is down. **Shipped 2026-09-18.** All three scope items landed. Item 1: `startSystemCapture` awaits `capture.start()` only through `boundedSystemCaptureStart` (10 s, `MeetingRecorder.systemStartBudgetSeconds`; a source fence holds the call count at one), a timeout emits `recorder`/`system_capture_start_timed_out` (`budget_s`, `is_retry`) and routes through `handleSystemAudioLost` with the loss dated to the attempt's start, so Retry becomes reachable, the HUD banner shows, and REC8's automatic retry follows 7 s later. Item 2: `SystemCaptureStartAttempt` is the pure ledger (`timedOut()` / `stopped()` before `completed()` yields `.discard`, `completed()` first yields `.adopt`); `SystemAudioCapture` consults it under one lock in the same critical section as the stream assignment, a discarded completion stops its stream at once, both delegate callbacks gate on it, and `stop()` now pre-empts a start that has not returned instead of no-oping on a nil stream. Item 3: `IdleStopBackstop.ingest` takes a `SystemChannel` (`.live(hasAudio:)` / `.down` / `.micOnly`), `.down` holds the streak and restarts it when the channel comes back, and `MeetingSessionController.backstopSystemChannel(health:levelDb:)` maps `MeetingRecorder.systemChannelHealth` into it; Screen-Recording-denied stays `.micOnly` and keeps today's behaviour. **Three decisions not in the spec:** the watchdog's gate is per attempt (`systemFires > systemFiresAtArm`) rather than `> 0`, because the counter runs across retries so every retry was already past the gate and a spin-up slower than 3 s was a false loss; both fire counters are zeroed at `start()` rather than `stop()`, because a buffer landing during teardown made the next recording start at 1 (114 of the 319 "system tap first fired" lines in the owner's log are that stop-time artefact) and the missing "first fired" line was this incident's own evidence; and a timed-out **retry** falls back to the banner without another automatic attempt (`autoRetry: !isRetry`), because a timed-out retry is the same outage continuing and re-entering ScreenCaptureKit every 17 s while it stays wedged piles up abandoned starts. +26 tests (19 in `SystemCaptureStartREC17Tests`, 7 in `IdleStopBackstopTests`; the 15 existing scenarios migrated mechanically). **What is not claimed:** no runtime test drives a real hung `startCapture()` (it needs a live SCStream, REC12's reason), so the recorder's timeout branch and the capture's late-completion path are pinned by the ledger, the bounded runner, and source fences, not by execution. `swift build` debug and release clean in the touched files, `swift test` **2976 passed, 1 skipped (VALOWN1), 0 failures** against a 2950 baseline on the same checkout, `truth_fences.py both` green. `README.md`, `docs/GUIDE.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` and one `daemon/CLAUDE.md` rule updated; `CONVENTIONS.md` unchanged (`recorder` is an existing category), `design/` untouched (the banner is the existing one). **Remainder is owner-only:** walkthrough steps 2 and 3, proposed weight complex (the hang cannot be provoked; it is instrumentation-then-wait across live calls), pending the owner's decision on moving the row. |

| [UX74](./tasks/UX74.md) | Every duration readout is the audio file's mtime, so a touched recording reads as hundreds of hours | UX | partial | P1 | Filed 2026-08-10 from the owner's 2026-08-04 notes, which asked for verification of an "impossible" Meeting time rail (Untagged 2406 h, Total 2469 h). It is a real arithmetic defect and the reported figures reproduce almost to the digit: `MeetingStore.swift:565-569` derives duration as the audio file's **mtime minus the stem's start time**, so summing the owner's 267 recordings gives **2473.9 h** against their reported 2469 h, and the Untagged bucket computes to **2407.1 h** against their 2406 h, while the real total audio is **75.8 h**. The rail is over by 33x. Five early stems whose actual audio is 5.7 to 9.0 seconds each contribute **2392.7 h, 96.7% of the headline figure**, because something later touched their `.wav`; all five have `workflow_name: null`, which is why Untagged absorbs nearly the whole artifact. The honest number (`audio_seconds`, present on 220 of 249 transcripts and exact for all five) is only read when the audio is **absent**, so the reliable source is skipped precisely when a file exists to be misread. Not just the rail: the same value labels the Library list row (`MeetingRow.swift:152`) and the detail header (`MeetingDetailView+Header.swift:346`), which read plausibly on most meetings and are why this survived. **P1 as a correctness defect on a shipped surface whose only output is these numbers; the owner may re-band, since nothing is lost and the fix is small.** Scope: prefer `audio_seconds`, then a header read, and demote mtime to the clamped last resort it always should have been. Explicitly not repairing the five files' mtimes (they are the fixture) and not a clamp alone. **Shipped 2026-08-18, engineering green; the remainder is the owner's walkthrough, and this row is waiting on approval to move rather than moved.** `buildMeeting` no longer stats the file for a length: a new `MeetingStore.duration` resolves the transcript's `audio_seconds` first, then the audio's own header, then the mtime span clamped to a positive interval, evaluated lazily so a present `audio_seconds` costs no header read and a readable header costs no stat. It stays inside the derivation `cacheable` governs, so a terminal row pays both reads once per signature change rather than once per scan. **4 new tests**, three of them red against the pre-fix code at exactly the reported scale (the touched-stem row returned 1960560 s, 544.6 h, where it now returns 9.0453125 s; the `MeetingStats.derive` fixture totalled 2010962 s, 558.6 h, against 4509.05 s now), and the fourth green on both sides by design: it is the fence against over-correcting, asserting that a row with audio, no transcript and an undecodable file still reports a length from mtime rather than blanking the label. `swift build` clean, `swift test` green, `truth_fences.py both` green, pipeline ruff / pyright / pytest green and untouched, zero U+2014 in the diff. **Two decisions named for challenge.** The header read reuses `AudioTranscoder.duration(of:)` rather than copying `WaveformPeaks`'s arithmetic as the spec proposed: that helper is already exactly the header-only read the spec describes (open, frames over sample rate, no decode), it is covered by `AudioTranscoderTests`, and a third copy of the same three lines is what the repo's one-home rule exists to prevent. And the header read is gated on `isFinalRecording`, so a still-recording row's capture intermediate falls through to mtime instead of being header-read: a WAV's frame count is only truthful once the file is closed, and that gate is what keeps the spec's own reason for retaining mtime intact. No ceiling was imposed on the mtime proxy, per the spec's rejection of a plausible-maximum clamp. `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `MeetingStore` bullet gained the derivation order; README, CONVENTIONS and GUIDE were checked and left alone, since none of them ever stated how a length was computed. **Owner-owed, and the clarification the gate asks for:** *what to do* is `./scripts/rebuild.sh` (left to the owner, deliberately not run from the session), then steps 2 to 5 of the walkthrough in [`tasks/UX74.md`](./tasks/UX74.md); *a pass* is the Meeting time rail reading roughly 76 h all-time instead of 2469 h, the five named stems reading as a few seconds each, and two normal recent meetings still reading right in both the list row and the detail header; *the cost* is one rebuild and a few minutes in the Library, with no live call and no waiting, which is the `simple` weight; *why a harness cannot* is step 4 alone, since the fix moves the value behind both per-meeting labels and a regression there would be silent, while steps 2 and 3 are pinned by the new tests over a synthetic fixture rather than over the owner's real 267 recordings. |

| [AUTO5](./tasks/AUTO5.md) | `get_transcript` cannot page a transcript, and silently swallows the `offset` a client reaches for | Automation | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-21 from the owner exercising AUTO3's MCP server in a Claude Code session: the client passed `offset`, got `offset: 0` back, said the parameter was being ignored, and left the tool to read the transcript `.md` off disk instead. Verified by probe, not inferred. `offset` is an **output** of `mcp_tools.get_transcript` (the computed window start, nonzero only when `around` matched) and is not an input at any layer; `mcp_serve.tool_specs()` advertises only `stem`, `around` and `window`, and the payload function raises `TypeError` on an `offset` keyword. The transport accepts it anyway and drops it in silence, because the `get_transcript` branch of `mcp_serve.dispatch` reads the three keys it knows and no `inputSchema` sets `additionalProperties: false`, so a wrong call returns a plausible right-looking answer instead of an error. That holds for all five tools. The substantive half is that the response is a pagination envelope (`offset`, `window`, `total_chars`, `truncated`) with no cursor input to act on it, so `truncated: true` is a dead end and the only positioning control is `around`, a substring the caller must already know appears in text it has not read. Leaving the tool is what costs: a direct file read skips `visibility.visible_stems` (leg 0's NDA and no-stance withholding) and `transcript_corrections.overlaid_markdown`, so quoted text is stale against the Library. Bounded honestly in the spec: that bypass needs the agent to hold its own file-read tool, so leg 0 is not broken and a bare MCP client cannot do it; what is damaged is that the guarded route is the one an agent gives up on. Not an AUTO3 regression, which shipped `get_transcript(stem, around, window)` exactly as filed. Scope: a position input wired through `tool_specs`, `dispatch` and `mcp_tools.get_transcript`, clamped and with its `around` composition written down; one decision on unknown arguments applied at the `dispatch` seam for all five tools (`window: 0` silently becoming 4000 rides along); tests, since `test_mcp_serve.py` pins the bounded window, the cap, `around` centering and the withheld-stem shape but nothing about argument handling. Owner-gated on one half only: a working `offset` turns `MAX_TRANSCRIPT_WINDOW` from a cap on total reach into a cap per call, which is the owner's call to make explicitly, though the spec records that the cap is already walkable via successive `around` phrases and wholly bypassable via the file read. Docs checked at filing and none appear owed (README, GUIDE and CONVENTIONS describe the surface without enumerating parameters). Spec in [`tasks/AUTO5.md`](./tasks/AUTO5.md). **Half shipped 2026-08-22.** The spec's own gate held: its walkthrough step 1 says the position input cannot be built until the owner picks a shape, and that the unknown-argument half can be built before it is answered, so only that half landed. Every `inputSchema` now carries `additionalProperties: false`, applied in a loop so a sixth tool inherits it, and `dispatch` rejects any argument the called tool does not declare, naming the key and listing what is accepted; the accepted set derives from the schemas (`TOOL_ARGUMENTS`) so check and advertisement cannot drift, and the rule sits at the seam rather than in one branch. The falsy-integer bug is fixed with `_int_or`: `window: 0` no longer becomes 4000, and the three `limit` sites were fixed with it, since they are the same line and the same wrong answer (a deviation from the spec, which named only `window`). +8 tests in `test_mcp_serve.py`, 6 measured red by reverting the check and the fix. Two of the new tests were wrong first and were corrected against real behaviour rather than the other way round: `window` reports the slice length on a short transcript, and `list_meetings` already floors `limit` at 1. ruff clean, pyright 0 errors, 1352 pytest green, truth fences green. `CONVENTIONS.md` gained the new client-visible `unknown_argument` payload (the spec's own trigger for owing it); `README.md` and `docs/GUIDE.md` re-checked and owe nothing, as the spec predicted. **Remainder, and it is a decision before it is code:** the owner picks the position-input shape (plain `offset`, a server-issued cursor, or no input plus honest `truncated` wording), because it changes `MAX_TRANSCRIPT_WINDOW` from a cap on reach to a cap per call. Then a session builds that half against the spec's remaining acceptance bars. |

| [DET6](./tasks/DET6.md) | Browser-tab meetings other than Meet are undetectable | Detection | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-07-20 off the DET2 close. Only Google Meet clears the plain-browser admission gate: `strongTitleMatchers` holds one entry (the Meet room-code pattern), and the URL-fragment disjunct reads the AX window title while browsers put the page title there and never the URL, so it effectively never fires. A tab running Teams, Webex or a Slack huddle matches weakly and is refused; Zoom-in-browser has no title matcher at all. P2 rather than P1 because the dogfood is Teams-native with 0 recorded browser meetings; re-band to P1 the moment a browser meeting is wanted. **Probe-first spike shipped 2026-08-18, measurement owner-owed.** Shipped: the admission decision lifted into a pure `MeetingSourceScanner.decideBrowserAdmission` (admit / noMeetingSignal / weakTitleOnly) so every case is testable without a live browser, and as a side effect one AX window walk per browser per scan instead of up to three; the refusal made visible as `detector.browser_tab_rejected` carrying bundle id, the weakly-matching title and a reason, which needed its own `Result.rejectedBrowserTabs` list for the same structural reason DET11's `unreadableNatives` did (both neighbouring events filter the candidate array, and a refused tab never enters it), read via `mp logs --action browser_tab_rejected`; an owner-run read-only probe `daemon/scripts/det6-browser-url-probe.swift` that refuses to capture or emit a verdict without an Accessibility grant, since an untrusted read is a false zero that agrees with the expected answer; and a pre-registered verdict tree in `docs/spikes/det6-browser-url-readability.md` (URL readable -> repoint the fragment check; title discriminating -> per-product strong matchers; neither -> close the leg as a permanent limitation). 13 tests added, split into unconditional fences (the two Meet admissions, and "Webex vs Zoom Comparison" which must stay refused in every branch) and pre-verdict pins of today's brand-token refusals. Remainder, all owner-gated on the probe: the measurement itself, whatever widening it names, and the two adjacent gaps that close with it (no Zoom `defaultTitleMatchers` entry, no Slack URL fragment). `strongTitleMatchers` deliberately NOT widened on a guess. Note the MIC7 interaction: its build gate is "a real browser meeting appears in the corpus", which this gap partly suppresses. Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: re-confirmed at HEAD (D011); the field pack has 1 refusal (a Teams join launcher) and no missed non-Meet browser call, so P2 stands; sequenced after DET16's count and DET24's B1, and the scheduled-Meet title gap is DET17's. |
| [DET10](./tasks/DET10.md) | DET1's mic-busy input has never produced a span, so the catch-all tier is inert and DET3's miss auditor reports a structural zero | Detection | partial | P2 | **Both legs that land whatever the verdict shipped 2026-08-15, and the probe is owner-owed.** `MeetingDiscoveryWatcher`'s nil-device branch emits `detector.mic_device_unavailable` (carrying the mic TCC status it saw) instead of returning silently, over the pure `MicInputAvailability.swift`; and `mp analyze-detection` no longer renders a dead input as a clean week, heading a zero-span window **"Mic-busy misses (not audited)"** rather than "(0)" and carrying the same distinction into `--json` as `mic_busy.input_status`. `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2419 tests 0 failures with 3 new, ruff / pyright / 1207 pytest green, CI5 fences green. **Remainder, both owner-gated:** the probe (`daemon/scripts/det10-mic-busy-probe.swift`, one command in the spec's owner walkthrough, needs the owner's own Terminal and a real call) has not been run, and the GO/NO-GO branch is deliberately unwritten pending its verdict, since writing the fix against the untested cause is how DET2 reached a NO-GO that DET8 later refuted. Verdict table A-D pre-registered in the spec before the probe ran. Original filing: 2026-08-03 by the audit band; proposed high, downgraded to medium because the root cause is unestablished and the path is one the owner has not needed. Verified across the full log (2026-05-18 to 2026-08-03): `mic_busy_started` **0**, `mic_busy_ended` **0**, against `candidate_dropped` **257** emitted from the same file on the same commit day, so the zero is structural rather than a routing artifact. `MeetingDiscoveryWatcher.swift:155` is the only producer, `MicBusySpanTracker.swift:56-59` opens a span only on `busy == true`, and `:239-242` means `MicInUseTier.decide` is never called. **The second-order effect is the worse one**: DET3 built `mp analyze-detection`'s mic-busy dimension to make silent detection false-negatives visible, and it reports zero misses because its own input is dead, the same false-zero shape as CAL1's EventKit reads. `README.md:575` promises the catch-all to the user. Two causes fit equally (a nil device at launch, silent and never retried at `:135`, or the property being structurally false for shared inputs), so this is probe-first: do not write a fix against the second without measuring, which is how DET2 reached a NO-GO that DET8 later refuted. Amended 2026-10-25 (observability band): the installed daemon logs CoreAudio no object with given ID 0 every 12 s since 2026-10-24 01:33:54 local (9,041 in two days, still 266 an hour on 2026-10-25), inferred to be sampleMicBusy on a launch-resolved, now-stale mic (LG-13). Confirm, re-resolve on disconnect or default-input change, emit mic_device_changed. Amended 2026-10-25 (performance review): the mic-busy KVO binds to `AVCaptureDevice.default(for: .audio)` once in `wireMicObserver` and never follows a default-input change, so the GO branch's re-resolve must also rebind on device change, closing the open span without firing `onMicBusyBundle(nil)` (which would stop a DET1 recording); MicGate's `HALVoiceActivityProbe` binds once per recording with the same gap, and that leg does not wait on the probe. Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: the work laptop rules out a nil device (0 mic_busy and 0 mic_device_unavailable rows in 114,553 events while process audio read true 420 times), so replace the AVCapture input with the HAL holder read rather than repair it (D002, D019, D050). |
| [DET12](./tasks/DET12.md) | Revive the start-side process-audio corroborator for native apps | Detection | partial | P2 | **Shipped 2026-08-18**, remainder is one owner-owed log read. Three seams, in the order the spec named them. **(1) The cache, which is the precondition rather than a tuning pass.** `ProcessAudioScanProbe` in `MeetingPipeCore/Lifecycle/Signals/`, beside DET8's `ProcessAudioSignal` and sharing its matcher, its `readIsRunningInput` and its `reduceIsRunningInput` rather than growing a second spelling of any of them. It differs only in caching shape: `beginScan` drops the snapshot, the first read of a pass takes a new one, and every later candidate matches against that one. Lazy, so a scan whose candidates are all outside the read set touches no HAL at all; and deliberately not held across scans, since a synchronous scan has no list-change listener and DET8 measured `replayd` taking a new object ID under the same PID nine minutes later. **(2) The native corroboration disjunct.** `MeetingSourceCandidate.Signals.processAudioIsRunningInput` at `Weights.processAudioIsRunningInput = 3` (the pre-DET2 value restored, not re-derived), counted by `MeetingSourceScorer.distinctSignalCount` and OR-ed by `hasCorroboratingSignal`. The native-only restriction is one mechanism rather than two: `MeetingSourceScanner.readsProcessAudio` is the single gate on who is read, and it asks `NativeLifecycleConfig.usesProcessAudio` rather than keeping a second list, so the start and end sides cannot disagree about which apps the read is trusted for. Browsers, PWAs, Webex and Slack all answer false; `decideBrowserAdmission` is untouched and the bare-brand-token gate is exactly as DET6 left it. **(3) The `shouldWalkControlAX` precondition**, restored as the fourth admission and the only one that is evidence of a live call rather than a guess that one is plausible. **The `isConfidentNativeMeeting` decision: process audio DOES count toward that higher bar**, via `distinctSignalCount` rather than a second count. The bar exists to reject signals that linger after a call, and this is the one signal measured not to (every Teams object read false 0 s and 11.4 s after the leave across DET8's two calls) while a stale Leave button is what DET5 was raised against; the one app known to hold the mic post-call, Webex, is excluded from the read entirely rather than discounted at the bar; and a separate count would have to stay in step with the one `pickBest`'s zero-evidence filter already uses, which has to count this signal or the DET11 payoff does not exist. So title plus audio now clears it, audio alone still does not. **The DET11 half is real but partial, stated plainly**: the read sits outside `collectSignals`' `axTrusted` gate and needs no Accessibility or Screen Recording grant, so a Teams or Zoom candidate on the mic under a denied grant now carries one real signal and is reported as a `candidate_dropped` with its evidence attached instead of as a zero-evidence `candidate_unreadable`. It does not make that machine detect a meeting, because one signal never clears `isConfidentNativeMeeting`; lowering that is a separate decision needing its own measurement. `discovery_shadow_pick` and `candidate_dropped` both gained a `process_audio_is_running_input` attribute (no new action, no new category). **22 tests added** (9 `ProcessAudioScanProbeTests`, 8 `MeetingSourceScannerTests`, 5 `MeetingSourceScorerTests`), including the counting-fake enumeration test with two candidates in one scan; `swift build` and `swift test` green, ruff clean, pyright 0 errors, pytest green, `truth_fences.py both` green. `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s detection bullet corrected, since "gates every native signal on AX trust" stopped being true; DET11's own corrected copy stays true and was not touched. **Remainder, owner-owed and not closable here**: the behavioural bar, read from `events.jsonl` on the owner's next Teams calls, that the scan attributes a process-audio signal to the Teams candidate at or before the scan producing the confident winner rather than only after recording started. DET8 measured 8.3 s and 2.1 s of headroom, so a start-side read that never precedes the winner means the wiring is wrong even with every test green. **Bar PASSED, read from the log 2026-08-19.** Four unstaged Teams calls in one day. The 15:03:16.348 scan picked Teams as confident winner carrying process_audio_is_running_input true on the winning scan itself, 547 ms ahead of the AX control walk. Better than the bar asked: the day is a natural experiment, 13 dropped scans with an identical AX signal set (title only, all controls false, audio false) against that one winner differing in exactly one bit, so DET12's acceptance case is observed on real data both ways round. The other two winners went to mute and leave with audio still false, which is the expected division of labour rather than a miss. A hypothesis formed on the first two calls, that the call UI always beats the mic so the clause might be unsatisfiable, is recorded as refuted by the third. **No engineering remains; the bar was the last item.** Spec in [`tasks/DET12.md`](./tasks/DET12.md). Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: on the work laptop each Zoom pickup waited 12 s, 64 s and 24 s for process audio, and audio alone never clears the confident-native bar, so decide whether sustained HAL input from a registered meeting process is the call (D015). |

| [DIST3](./tasks/DIST3.md) | The installed app cannot be found by Spotlight | Distribution | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-04, observed during UX54's walkthrough. `~/Applications/MeetingPipe.app` exists and Launch Services knows it (it enumerates as a peer and `open` resolves it), but Spotlight reports "unknown indexing state" for the home volume, so typing "MeetingPipe" into Spotlight finds nothing and the owner has to navigate to the folder. The root cause is machine-level rather than an app defect, which is why UX54 declined to fold it in, but the fix that helps is ours: `install.sh` does not run `mdimport` on the bundle after installing it, so a volume whose index is in a bad state never gets told the app is there. Scope is one `mdimport` call at the end of the install path, non-fatal if it fails (a missing index is an annoyance, not a reason to fail an otherwise good install), plus a `mp doctor` check that reports whether the bundle is indexed so the next occurrence is diagnosable rather than re-derived. Setup ease is explicitly in scope per the CLAUDE.md carve-out (DOC10), so this is not selling machinery. **Shipped 2026-08-18, `partial` on the owner walkthrough. One filing correction first, and it moves which half is the fix:** Scope item 1 was already in the tree. `scripts/install.sh` has run `mdimport` on the bundle since 2026-04-30 (`17d947d9`), directly after its `ditto`, and the comment above it already claimed it forces an immediate reindex. So the 2026-08-04 observation was made **with that call in place**, which means asking Spotlight did not help on this volume and the mdimport half was never the fix. That corroborates this task's own "the root cause is machine-level" reading rather than contradicting it, and it makes item 2, diagnosability, the whole of the value. What actually shipped: the existing call now **says which of the three things happened** (mdimport absent, asked and succeeded, asked and failed) instead of `>/dev/null 2>&1 \|\| true`, which is what the Acceptance asked for and is why nothing in the install output ever distinguished them; the overstated comment is corrected; `scripts/rebuild.sh` gets the same ask, since it does write into `~/Applications/MeetingPipe.app` and is the fast path the owner runs most often, so a missing index gets another chance on every rebuild; and `mp doctor` gains `check_app_index`, which asks `mdfind -name MeetingPipe.app` and reports four states rather than two. The four are deliberate: **"Spotlight answered and does not know it" and "Spotlight could not be asked" are different facts**, and collapsing the second into the first is how a void check gets banked as a real one (the DET2 lesson), so mdfind being absent, exiting non-zero, or raising all report `[WARN] index state unknown` and never `[FAIL]`. Remediation stops where this task's out-of-scope list says: it offers `mdimport` and points `mdutil -s /` at the volume's status, and never `mdutil -E`, because repairing the index is the owner's machine-level decision. 7 tests added, one per state plus the stale-other-copy case. Verified: ruff / pyright / pytest green, `swift build` + `swift test` green (untouched), truth fences green. Docs: README's build-steps line claimed the ditto was "so Spotlight can launch it", which is exactly the claim this task disproves, so it now says asking is all the install can do; plus a README troubleshooting entry and a plain-language `docs/SETUP.md` one, that file being where a setup-ease failure belongs (DOC10). **One open question for the owner, not resolved here:** `install.sh`'s closing text says of the CLI tools "Everything they do is also in the app", and `DiagnosticsWindow` reads its signals in Swift rather than shelling out to `mp doctor`, so this check has no in-app equivalent and that sentence is now slightly false. **That paragraph was wrong and is struck in the spec, 2026-08-19: the check already has an in-app surface.** `DoctorRunner` spawns `mp doctor` and streams its whole output into a Preferences sheet, and `check_app_index()` runs in that command's main, so the Spotlight result and its mdimport fix line render at Preferences, Integrations, Run doctor. The mistaken claim named `DiagnosticsWindow`, the UX20 event-log viewer, which is a different surface that genuinely has no doctor in it. **De-CLI decision closed on the corrected facts: no code needed.** Recorded and not filed: the sheet's only entry point is the Integrations header, an odd home for an install problem, which is a discoverability note about every doctor check. **Remainder: the owner walkthrough in [`tasks/DIST3.md`](./tasks/DIST3.md)**, steps 2 to 4. |

| [DV5](./tasks/DV5.md) | Research what the Facts rail should become, or whether it should exist | Intelligence | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-10 at the owner's request from their 2026-08-04 notes: "facts is barely useful, just endless list with all action items, makes not so many sense ... perhaps we can make some 'dashboard' or 'insights' instead ... or remove it. research it". Follow-on to DV1. Measured over their 226 summaries on 2026-08-10 so the pickup starts from numbers: 1374 actions of which **16 are resolved (1.2%)**, 1358 open, **75% carry an owner but only 4% carry a due date**, 309 decisions, median 6 open actions per meeting. The daemon's own `action_clusters_built` collapses 1300 actions to 838 clusters, so the list is largely repetition. Two readings follow. A backlog closing 1.2% of its items is not a list anyone works from, which makes **Remove a serious candidate rather than a rhetorical one**; and 4% date coverage means **no time-shaped view is buildable at all** (no burndown, overdue chart, or velocity), which is the most useful thing the measurement rules out and should stop a plausible dashboard proposal before it is designed. Owners at 75% and the denser decisions corpus are what remains. Scope is a research pass ending in a named Reshape / Replace / Remove recommendation, probe-first via `/spike DV5` with thresholds pre-registered, **not** a licence to build a dashboard. It must first get the owner's real questions in their own words (the rail's failure is that it answers "what open items exist" and nobody asked that), clarify what "move as separate tab -> research" meant, and say what any proposal adds beyond the shipped People (DV3), Meeting time (AI8) and `mp ask` surfaces. Standing constraints: AI5's classifier NO-GO, AI9's corrections-based shape, the AI/DV egress rule, the de-CLI rule. Explicitly not backfilling due dates (4% is how people talk, not a data bug), not AI7 or AI10, and not using AI8's current numbers as input since UX74 is an open defect in them. **Measurement half done 2026-08-19, recommendation blocked on the owner by the spec's own design.** Analysis at [`../spikes/dv5-facts-rail.md`](../spikes/dv5-facts-rail.md), with the reproduce command recorded and checked to actually run. Corpus re-measured at 249 summaries (226 at filing). **Two findings move the task before any design exists.** First, the resolution rate is not low, it is zero: resolved is still exactly 16 across a corpus that gained 150 open actions in nine days, so the mark-done control was used zero times over the window rather than 1.2% of the time. Second, and this corrects the spec itself: the filed table's "75% of open actions have an owner" counts string presence, not people. Measured by content, 570 of the 1126 owned open actions are diarization artifacts (357 bare numbers, 127 `THEM-*`, 86 `Speaker N`), so only **556, 36.9%, name a human**. The one field the spec offered as a foundation is half what it credits. Two fields were never measured at filing and are now: 746 open questions (the largest unsurfaced corpus, against 327 decisions), and `group` persisted on 0% of actions, since AI7's clustering is computed at read time. **The survey closes off both obvious reshapes in advance:** anything time-shaped fails on 4.6% date coverage as filed, and anything owner-shaped both fails the field floor and duplicates People (DV3), which already renders per-person open commitments off the same `[ActionCluster]` Facts renders. Verdict thresholds T1 to T5 are pre-registered in the doc, written while the owner's answers were still unknown so they cannot be tuned to the answer, and Remove is the default when nothing survives them. On today's corpus only the action text, `resolved`, decisions and open questions clear the T1 floor, so a Reshape would be about decisions or questions rather than about actions, which is the opposite of what the rail does now. Also added the measured finding to `pipeline/docs/gotchas.md`, since `Facts.swift` renders `action.owner` verbatim while `PeopleRail` matches it against enrolled names and degrades safely. **Blocked on the owner:** walkthrough step 1's two questions (what would you ask of 249 meetings, in your words; and what "facts move as separate tab" meant), which the spec says cannot be derived or guessed. Not owner-verification, so the row stays here rather than moving. |

| [FEAT17](./tasks/FEAT17.md) | Spike: does FEAT14's near-miss suggestion ever fire on the owner's real library | Feature | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-18 as [FEAT14](./tasks/FEAT14.md)'s remainder, on the owner's decision that day to build the probe **instead of spending a re-transcribe**. `/spike` shaped, with the thresholds pre-registered in the spec before any number exists. The question is one thing only: how many of the ~207 summarized meetings would have offered a suggestion. It needs a probe because **FEAT14's in-app check passes either way by design** ("no invented guess" is a correct outcome), so no owner check can tell "works" from "never fires on this library", and the second is live: the roster holds **11 people carrying 1 or 2 samples each**, exactly the regime where scores sit **below** `SUGGEST_MIN = 0.50` rather than in the 0.50-to-`MATCH_MIN` 0.65 band a suggestion needs. Four things close a priori from code, chief among them that no meeting on disk carries a `near_misses` key (finalize writes schema 2; every meeting predates FEAT14), so the probe **recomputes** `match_detail` rather than reading one, and that the margin gate gates the suggestion too, so margin-blocked declines get their own bin or the next task points at the wrong constant. Pre-registered: **GO** at 21+ meetings (10%, twice the ~5% auto-naming reaches today), **MARGINAL** at 3 to 20, **CLOSE** at 2 or fewer, with a second reading over why the declines decline. Read-only, no re-transcribe, no UI, no production change, no TCC grant needed. Spec in [`tasks/FEAT17.md`](./tasks/FEAT17.md). **Measured 2026-08-19. Verdict GO: 31 of 83 meetings (37 percent) would have offered a suggestion, against a pre-registered bar of 21.** Analysis at [`../spikes/feat17-roster-near-miss-coverage.md`](../spikes/feat17-roster-near-miss-coverage.md); probe at [`../spikes/feat17_roster_near_miss.py`](../spikes/feat17_roster_near_miss.py). **The spec's "why it cannot be answered in this harness" section is wrong about this environment**, which is why there is a number rather than an instrument awaiting one: the library and roster are ordinary file reads needing no TCC grant, as the spec itself notes, so the probe was built to spec, verified read-only, and then simply run. The owner's walkthrough step 2 is already done and cost them nothing. **Two of the four a-priori closures needed correcting.** Closure 1 said every sidecar predates FEAT14 and is schema 1; measured, 80 are schema 1 and **3 are schema 2**, though its conclusion survives because **zero** carry a `near_misses` key, so recomputation was still the only route. And the denominator is not FEAT14's 207: the corpus holds 249 summaries but only **83 embeddings sidecars**, since one is written only when diarization produced embeddings. GO clears on both the absolute count and the percentage, so neither correction changes the verdict. **The secondary reading fires at exactly its boundary**, `below_floor` 80.1 percent against an 80 percent mark, and the substance is not in doubt regardless: median top-candidate similarity across 504 labels is 0.481, a hair under `SUGGEST_MIN`, which is the weak-centroid regime FEAT14 predicted. So the feature is worth having and is not the bottleneck; enrolment is. `margin_blocked` at 8.9 percent is well under its mark, so roster ambiguity is not a live problem at 15 people. **No constant was touched**, per the spike contract. 14 tests added over the probe's two pure functions (binning, verdict mapping), which is where a silent mistake would misreport a correct measurement. Read-only proved by copying 25 sidecars, running, and confirming every sha and mtime byte-identical with nothing created; an empty directory prints `NOTHING SCANNED` and exits 2 so it can never read as CLOSE. ruff clean, pyright 0 errors, pytest 1324 passed, truth fences green. **Placement discrepancy, as the spec asked:** the probe went to `docs/spikes/` beside its doc, following the four existing probes, while `.claude/commands/spike.md` step 5 says `daemon/scripts/` or `pipeline/`. Precedent won; the command's line is the one that is out of step. **Owner-owed, and it is a decision rather than a check:** GO means FEAT14's remainder is only its in-app eyeball, so FEAT14's row should move to `owner-verification.md`, which needs the owner's approval per that file's gate. Then the close word. |

| [HYG8](./tasks/HYG8.md) | Three more dead-link mechanisms HYG6 did not cover, plus two accreted conventions in the backlog's own files | Hygiene | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-18 off the ten-task batch (`20e66c7`..`b122e00`); everything here sat outside HYG6's named scope after it swept 88 links. Measured against `b122e00` with a resolver that ignores links inside code spans: **24 dead relative links in `docs/backlog/`** (25 counting `END9.md`'s, which is HYG7's), in three groups with three causes. **(1)** A *third* mechanism, distinct from HYG6's two: archiving a spec kills every citation **of** it elsewhere, since `tech-task.md` step 8 rewrites the archiving spec's own links and nothing rewrites the links into it. Ten in `q6-final.md` (`./tasks/UX57.md`, `UX58`, `UX52`, `UX49`, `LANG2`, `UX44`, `AI12`, `UX71`, `./LOCAL15.md`, a second `./UX58.md`), and, not previously reported, one in a **live** spec: `DET12.md`'s `[DET11](DET11.md)`. UX72 measured 11 flags before and after its own archival, an identical set, so today's four archivals added none. **(2)** 10 one-level depth errors across three live specs: `CAL3` 7, `AUTO1` 2, `DIAR5` 1. Not `DOC13`: its four apparent hits are quotations of `docs/GUIDE.md`'s links inside backticks, which is why any fence here must strip code spans (a naive pass reports 28 and is wrong by exactly those four). **(3)** 3 in `q4-final.md` pointing at deleted source files. The rest of the repo is clean; the only other flag anywhere is the `meetingpipe://` deeplink example, which is correct prose. Plus two accreted conventions the finders deliberately left: the `Bands:` line names 20 IDs of which **17 are archived** and 2 are live, and ARCH6 read it as a live index while CI6 read it as the 2026-08-03 audit's frozen record (CI6's reading is the agreed one, so the fix is one deliberate pass deciding what it is for, not pruning one name); and `q6-final.md` carries **two** entry-header styles, 132 bold against 28 heading, with three of today's ten landings using the heading form, so it is an unwritten rule rather than one outlier. Also folds in UX72's run-on hazard: a `**[DONE]` line with no blank line above it renders as one paragraph and is invisible in a marker-free diff, exactly 2 instances, both in `q6-final.md`. Plus one instance of group 1's mechanism reached by a different route: FEAT14's row records that `pipeline/CLAUDE.md`'s Build + test block lists `uv run --extra dev pyright`, which `46e9d28` changed to `--extra dev --extra mcp` the same day, so a dated ship-note finding now describes a command that file does not list; the repair is a "fixed by `46e9d28`" clause, not a rewrite (the HYG6 and LOCAL14 convention), and it matters because it becomes an unqualified false claim about a live file the moment FEAT14 archives. `END9.md`'s dead link is [`HYG7`](./tasks/HYG7.md)'s, not this row's. Spec in [`tasks/HYG8.md`](./tasks/HYG8.md). **Items 1, 2, 3 and 5 plus the run-on fix shipped 2026-08-19; item 4 is the owner's.** **Walkthrough step 1, before and after:** the resolver reported **24 dead links** over `docs/backlog/**/*.md` and reports **0** now. That is 24 rather than the filed 25 because HYG7 landed first and repaired END9's, which was never this task's. All three groups repaired as the spec directed. **Group 1 needed a judgement the spec could not pre-make:** its ten q6-final citations point at specs that archived **into q6-final itself**, so pointing them at `q6-final.md` as the spec suggested would be a self-link, and an anchor over a 5,519-line file rots on the first title edit. They keep the id as text and drop the navigation, the reader already being in the file that holds the entry. The one cross-file case, DET12 citing DET11, took the `../q6-final.md` form DET12 already uses nine times. Group 2's ten depth errors are path repairs; group 3's three keep the file name and drop the link, since those sources are deleted and the archive is a record. **Both run-ons fixed** (C7 and UX32, both in q6-final, zero in the other three archives). **Item 2, the `Bands:` line, took the walkthrough's stated default: frozen** as the 2026-08-03 audit's record, said so in the line, with CI6's reasoning about why pruning one name makes a stale list worse. Re-measured first: of the ids it names, most have archived, DET9 is in owner-verification, and only DET10 and FEAT14 were still active. **Item 3 took its default too:** the bold form (132 against 28) is now named in "Marking a task done", the 28 heading entries are deliberately not restyled, and the blank-line-before-a-bold-header requirement is written down beside it since that is what produced both run-ons. **Item 5:** the `46e9d28` clause is appended to FEAT14's row with the original finding intact, and the commit was checked rather than taken on trust. **No production code touched**, `scripts/truth_fences.py` included. Truth fences green, dash guard clean. **Item 4 shipped 2026-08-19 on the owner's yes** (walkthrough step 4 answered directly): `scripts/truth_fences.py` gains a `links` mode covering dead relative links and UX72 run-on `**[DONE]` entries, with fenced blocks and inline code spans blanked before extraction so a spec can still quote a broken link. CI already invokes the script with no argument, so `both` picks it up with no workflow edit. 231 files, 709 links, 597 resolved, 0 findings. Verified red first against a four-case probe and against three mutants, each of which also turned the real tree red. Pinned by 17 tests in `pipeline/tests/test_link_fence.py`. Corrected in passing: the root CLAUDE.md verify row claimed a 12 KB budget; the real caps are 800 B per entry and 24 KB whole-file. Correction to this row's own item-1 note: not one `meetingpipe://` occurrence is a link target, so the scheme skip is exercised by the 80 `https:` targets, not by them.
