| [LOCAL18](./tasks/LOCAL18.md) | Nobody has seen the free-RAM watchdog fire, and its floor may be unreachable | Local models | new | P2 | Split out of LOCAL16 on 2026-07-31 by owner decision, on the VALID1-to-EGRESS1 precedent that an unmeasured leg is split rather than dressed up as a pass. LOCAL16's **tier refusal is verified** (Preferences annotations, `mp doctor`, and the refusal copy all confirmed by the owner that day) and it is the protection that would have stopped the documented 14B-on-32GB incident. The **runtime watchdog is not**: it has never fired in the entire event-log history, and an attempt to make it fire failed, with `memory_pressure -p 8 -y 45` reporting "Run out of allocable memory" while still at 65% by its own reckoning and the summarize completing normally. The doubt is about the metric rather than the code: `free_pct` sums `free`, `inactive`, `speculative` and `purgeable` (`resource_guard.py:309`) for a documented and sound reason (macOS keeps `Pages free` near zero, so a floor on free alone would fire constantly), but `inactive` and `purgeable` are what macOS reclaims **under** pressure, so the sum stays high exactly while the machine copes by compressing and swapping; during the test `vm_stat` showed 10.1M swapouts and ~7.5 GB in the compressor while the metric read 65% free. Scope splits in two: whether the abort path works at all, answerable safely today by raising the `summarization.local_memory_floor_pct` knob so any run trips it; and whether 10% is reachable, which needs an instrumented heavy run rather than synthetic allocation. Owner may re-band: this is the backstop under a P1's "never hangs the machine" promise, and P2 assumes the verified tier refusal carries the known case. Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: the 4,000-token tier ceilings sit below the 4,004 to 4,101-token fixed prompt, so 7B on 16 GB and 14B on 24 GB get the native 28,768 budget, and the watchdog samples only before a request (F079). |
| [LOCAL20](./tasks/LOCAL20.md) | Cancelling a model download reports it as failed, and a failed download has no exit but Retry | Local models | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-10 from the owner's 2026-08-04 notes ("there's still indication of failed download that I cannot discard. I can only click retry"). **The banner was manufactured by their own backend switch, proved by one line in their `daemon.log`:** `[2026-08-04T15:00:14.626Z] model prefetch terminated rc=15 state=failed(... "prefetch exited 15")`. `rc=15` is SIGTERM, which is what `ModelDownloadSupervisor.cancel()` sends; the prefetch had run three healthy minutes from 14:57:02Z and was killed 99 s before the 18:01:53 screenshot. Chain: a config persist calls `ensureModelPrefetchIfNeeded`, `prefetchDecision` returns `.cancelAndIdle`, and the apply site correctly cancels then pushes `.idle`, which is why this looks impossible from that file alone. But `cancel()` (`ModelDownloadSupervisor.swift:139`) never touches `state`, so it stays `.downloading`, and `handleTermination` (`:250`) takes **no identity check against `self.process` and no cancel marker**, sees a non-zero exit, and synthesizes `.failed`, firing the observer and putting the banner back. Then nothing clears it: `.completed` auto-collapses on a timer (`StatusBarController.swift:255`) and `.failed` has no such path, no dismiss exists, and Retry re-downloads the model the owner just moved away from. Same hole makes `ensure`'s replace-in-flight path able to report the *previous* model as failed over the new one's `.downloading`. Second-order: `"↓ failed"` outranks the `Processing (N)` clause while idle, so a phantom failure indefinitely suppresses real queue state in the menu-bar title. Scope: an explicit cancelled marker plus a process-identity check, then a dismiss and a rule that a `.failed` for a no-longer-configured model is stale. Keep a real, current failure visible. Worth fixing before LOCAL13's bench exercises the replace-in-flight path across ~33 GB of candidates. **Engineering shipped 2026-08-19; the remainder is the owner walkthrough only.** Both defects fixed. **1, the cancel.** The spec offered two routes and named the second as preferable; it is the one taken, and the `ensure` interaction is why. The pure `ModelDownloadSupervisor.terminationOutcome(isTrackedProcess:state:terminationStatus:)` returns `.ignore` for any process the supervisor is no longer tracking, and `handleTermination` returns **before** its teardown rather than after. That ordering is the part worth reading: the handler also nils `process` and `stdoutHandle` and clears `currentModelId`, so an abandoned prefetch could tear down the download that replaced it, not merely mislabel it. `cancel()` also now leaves `.idle` instead of `.downloading`, so the supervisor stops believing work is in flight; both halves are wanted rather than either alone. **2, the exit that is not Retry.** `.failed` gained `dismissible`, rendered as a `Dismiss` row under the retry row, wired through `ConfigRefreshCoordinator.dismissModelDownloadFailure` so Retry and Dismiss share one collaborator. The staleness rule the spec preferred falls out of the cancel fix rather than needing its own reconciliation pass: `.cancelAndIdle` cancels, and a `.failed` for a model being switched away from does not survive that; pinned by its own test. One seam added to production for testing, an `init(state:)` defaulted to `.idle`, because reaching `.failed` honestly needs a network failure; injection rather than a test-only setter, matching how `ConfigRefreshCoordinator` already takes its supervisor. 11 tests added, including the second-order cost the spec named: a phantom failure suppressed `Processing (N)` in the menu-bar title indefinitely, so clearing it has to give that clause back. `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2788 tests 0 failures, truth fences and dash guard clean, no pipeline change. Docs: `README.md` (its download paragraph said Retry was the only affordance), `ARCHITECTURE.md`, and `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` for the reusable lesson, that a terminating subprocess must prove it is still the tracked one, with the owner's `rc=15` line as its evidence. `docs/GUIDE.md` checked and unaffected: its Retry recipes are about the Library's failed-meeting rows, a different surface. No design mock models the menu-bar download row at all, so there was nothing to re-mock; `MenuBarDropdown`'s wider lag is already DSN30's. **Owner-owed:** the walkthrough, proposed weight `complex` rather than `simple` because step 2 pulls several GB and asks the owner to wait past the 99-second mark the original defect appeared at, and step 3 wants Wi-Fi off; no meeting or call audio is involved, so neither weight fits cleanly and the owner should pick. Needs the owner's approval before the row moves to `owner-verification.md`. |

| [PERF8](./tasks/PERF8.md) | Naming a speaker re-derives the whole library from disk, so one pick costs 2-5 seconds | Performance | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-18 from the owner's report that picking a name in the transcript takes 2-5 s and sometimes freezes the UI. Measured the same day against the owner's library (287 stems, 2263 files, 18 GB): every disk step is cheap (overlay write 2 ms, full transcript re-parse 17 ms, library rescan 30 ms, and no subprocess runs on the reassign path at all), so the cost is everything that surrounds the write. One pick writes the sidecar synchronously on main, re-reads and re-parses the whole transcript before the row repaints, trips the directory watcher with its own write (no self-write suppression exists anywhere in the daemon), and 500 ms later bumps the single global `MeetingStore.revision`, which wakes eight library-wide consumers. Scope: give the speaker overlay the in-memory optimistic model `saveCorrection` already uses for text edits, coalesce the write off-main, and stop the app rescanning for sidecars it authored. **Amended 2026-08-18 on the owner's sudden-shutdown question**, which exposed a live data-loss defect the first draft had waved out of scope: `SpeakerLabelStore.write` removes the real sidecar before renaming the temp over it, so a death in that window loses every name ever assigned to that meeting rather than just the edit being saved, and nothing ever reads the orphaned `.tmp`. Same shape in `TranscriptCorrectionStore` and `CorrectionStore`; `WorkflowStore.persist` already does it correctly with `replaceItemAt`. Atomic replacement plus a flush from `flushAndExit()` is now scope item (0) and a precondition for deferring any write. The unaccounted seconds are hypothesised to be SwiftUI relayout of the 1243-row transcript list; pinning that with an owner-run profiler capture is step 1 and the rest of the work is gated on it. **Scope item (0), the crash-safety precondition, shipped 2026-08-19. Items (1) to (4) are deliberately NOT started:** the spec gates them on a profiler capture ("do not start writing the fix before that capture exists"), and that capture is walkthrough step 2, owner-run. Item (0) is not gated on it, because its justification is independent of where the latency is: it is a data-loss window, not a performance redundancy, and the spec makes it a precondition that lands first. **What shipped:** all four writers replaced `write(temp)` + `removeItem(dest)` + `moveItem` with `data.write(to:options:.atomic)`, which already does temp-and-rename and has no window where the destination is absent. **The spec named three stores; there were four.** A sweep found the identical shape in `PipelineFailureSidecar.write`, which the spec's three-store scoping missed because its rationale was irreplaceable hand-made data and a failure sidecar is regenerable. Fixed anyway: the shape is strictly worse than the one-liner, it is the same four lines, and losing that sidecar is still user-visible (a failed meeting reads as clean in the Needs you rail and loses its retry affordance). Flagged rather than done silently, since it widens the spec's stated scope by one file. Also corrected four doc comments that claimed crash safety this shape did not have. **An honesty note on the tests, which the acceptance's wording does not survive contact with:** it asks for a test that "simulates a failure after the temporary file is written" and fails against `main`. That window opens between two operations inside one function and is not observable from outside the process, and the obvious way to force a failure, an unwritable directory, fails at the *first* step, where the old code also preserves the previous file. So the regression guard is a source fence over the four writers, which genuinely fails against `HEAD` (all four carried `moveItem(at: temp)`), with behavioural tests kept as a check on the property rather than as the guard. 4 tests added. `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2826 tests 0 failures, truth fences and dash guard clean, no pipeline change. `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` carries the lesson, including why the fence is a grep. Walkthrough step 1 done: A12's dir-level watcher constraint re-read before touching anything, and it is consistent with item (2)'s plan to suppress at the writer rather than the watch. **Owner-owed and blocking:** the `sample` capture. With MeetingPipe running and a long meeting's Transcript tab open, run `sample <pid> 5 -f /tmp/perf8.txt` and reassign a line while it samples, then hand the file back. About a minute, and it decides whether the SwiftUI-layout hypothesis stands or the spec gets amended before any of (1) to (3) is written. Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: once item 2 suppresses self-written rescans, every app writer must publish directly. |

| [PIPE18](./tasks/PIPE18.md) | PIPE11's duplicate-click coalescing covers one enqueue path of three, so seven Retry clicks queued seven full pipelines | Pipeline | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-14, owner-reported the same day with a screenshot reading `Processing 7`: "I clicked retry for 7 times and it put in queue 7 times ... I should not be able to put in queue something that is already there." Their event log has the whole burst on `20260813-170019`, a 25 m 45 s meeting: `retry_requested` + `pipeline_queued` at 11:57:05 (depth 1, started immediately), :10 (2), :16 x3 (3, 4, 5), :17 x2 (6, 7), then six manual `pipeline_dequeued` at 11:57:24-25 about 150 ms apart, then `pipeline_succeeded` at 11:58:11 for the run the **first** click started. So the first click worked, the run took 66 s, and every later click was made while the wanted retry was already running; the clicks accelerate (5 s, 6 s, three in one second) which is the shape of pressing harder, not of queueing on purpose. Uncleaned, six more `mp run-all` passes would have re-transcribed and re-summarized a 25-minute recording six times, each overwriting the last, at six paid calls on their `anthropic` default. **The guard exists and covers the wrong path:** `SinkDispatcher` has four enqueue entry points and only `enqueueLibraryRun` coalesces (PIPE11, identity `(verb, stem, backend)`, emitting `pipeline_coalesced`), while `enqueue(file:summaryMode:)` (`.full`, what Retry uses), `enqueueRetranscribe`, and the private `enqueue(job:)` they all funnel through have none. PIPE11's own comment names the 2026-07-23 incident it was built for. Not PIPE11's race, since the lane is serialized; the harm is redundant work, cost, and a final row state set by whichever identical run finished last. **The UI half is why seven clicks happened:** `runRetry` (`SummaryTab.swift:494`) answers success with `break` while a failure gets a modal, and none of the three Retry buttons consults queue state, so the pane offers `Retry pipeline` about 200 points below a header already rendering `Queued: Reprocess` with a working Remove. Scope: move the coalesce into the shared private `enqueue` keyed on stem so a fifth path cannot forget it, and point the retry affordance at the `LibraryProcessing.presentation` state its own header already uses. Ship both; item 2 stops the burst, item 1 makes it harmless. Explicitly not the serialization, not UX28's Remove, not UX69, and not why that meeting failed originally. The same screenshot is also a sharper instance of UX77. **Engineering shipped 2026-08-19; the remainder is the owner walkthrough only. Both halves shipped, as the spec required.** **1, coalescing.** Taken the way the spec preferred: the guard moved into the private `enqueue(job:completion:)` all four paths funnel through, rather than being copied onto the two that lacked it, so a fifth entry point inherits it instead of having to remember. `SinkDispatcher.isAlreadyQueued` is pure beside it and PIPE11's own `contains` was folded onto it, so there is one rule rather than two. It coalesces rather than replaces, per the spec's explicit call, and it reads only the live queue, so the legitimate second retry after a second failure still works. **One correction to the spec's identity, caught by the existing suite:** the spec says "identity is the stem for both kinds", and a first draft keyed on `file.deletingPathExtension().lastPathComponent`, which reads as obviously right because one directory holds every recording under a timestamp stem. `SinkDispatcherTests` failed immediately: those tests build per-test temp dirs each holding a `clip.wav`, so a stem-keyed guard collapsed two unrelated recordings into one. `.full` and `.retranscribe` are only ever enqueued with the recording URL, so the file itself is stricter and sufficient; `.libraryRun` keeps `(verb, stem, backend)` and never consults the file, which is what lets it anchor on the transcript while the queue holds audio. **2, the affordance.** `RetryAffordance.decide` reads the very `ProcessingPresentation` the detail header's queued line is built from, so the pane and the header cannot disagree again; the failed pane now shows the in-flight label with a spinner instead of offering the button. This is the half that stops the burst, and the spec is right that coalescing alone would only have made it harmless. 14 tests added (8 over the predicate, 5 over the affordance, 1 pinning the stem helper as log vocabulary rather than the guard's key). `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2802 tests 0 failures, truth fences and dash guard clean, no pipeline change. Docs: `README.md`, `docs/GUIDE.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, and `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` for the reusable lesson, that a guard belongs at the funnel rather than at the call sites, with the identity-strength trap recorded beside it. `pipeline_coalesced` was already in the CONVENTIONS event table from PIPE11 and stays green; its attributes gained `kind` and `queue_depth` and keep `verb`/`backend` for library runs. **Owner-owed:** the walkthrough, proposed weight `simple` (make a meeting fail, press Retry, then press it repeatedly; no live call). Needs the owner's approval before the row moves to `owner-verification.md`. |

| [REC8](./tasks/REC8.md) | Self-healing capture | Recording | partial | P2 | Implementation shipped + unit-tested: one automatic ~7 s system-audio retry then the manual banner; an `IOPMAssertPreventUserIdleSystemSleep` assertion held across recording (released in stop + termination); an `NSWorkspace.didWakeNotification` re-arm; and the tap watchdog un-gated from `engine.isRunning` via a pure `TapWatchdogPolicy` (3 tests). | [REC15](./tasks/REC15.md) | A caught installTap raise costs the whole meeting's mic, and spins at 1 Hz while it does | Recording | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-19 from the field, by the owner reporting the toast "Could not start recording: Audio engine failed to start: installTap raised on the input node". REC12's barrier is working (three raises that day, zero crash reports); what it did not cost out is what the survivor does next, and both halves were wrong. **The toast** is an auto-start lost to a race rather than a state: `installTap` re-reads the hardware format after we read it, the AirPods had gone back to 48000 Hz while the tap was requested at 24000, and the owner's own manual retry 22 s later read 48000 and recorded 22 minutes. Nothing tried that on their behalf. **The third raise**, 18 minutes after REC12's ship note was written, is the larger one: it landed mid-recording, and since every terminal return from `recoverCapture` leaves the engine stopped while REC8 deliberately un-gated the 1 Hz tap watchdog from `engine.isRunning`, recovery was re-entered every second and refused every time (12 consecutive refusals, ended only by the owner hanging up). The `.timedOut` branch's comment asserted the watchdog "stops once isRunning is false", which stopped being true at REC8 and is why the spin looked impossible. **Shipped 2026-08-19, three changes.** A raised start retries once on a fresh engine (raises only, never format refusals: the barrier's own doc draws that line, and a refusal is a state where a raise is a race); `recoverCapture` replaces a condemned engine and retries once the same way, re-applying voice processing and re-reading the format on the new node, so the mic comes back mid-meeting; and `TapWatchdogPolicy.decide` gained a `micAbandoned` input, set at once when a fresh engine also raises and otherwise after a budget of 5 consecutive failed recoveries. A bound rather than a latch, because the other four terminal returns can succeed on the next attempt. A real device-change notification still reaches recovery: it carries new information about the route, where the watchdog's tick carries none. 5 tests, measured red by deleting the guard. Three new `recorder` actions (`mic_tap_start_retried`, `mic_tap_recovery_retried`, `mic_capture_abandoned`), no new category. The two retry paths are not behaviourally tested, for REC12's reason (a live engine bound to a real input device, which neither CI nor this runner has); the pure half that spun is. Spec in [`tasks/REC15.md`](./tasks/REC15.md). **P2 by the REC10 precedent** (loss on a path reached only after another failure); the owner may re-band, since the outcome is a whole meeting uncaptured. **Owner-owed:** walkthrough steps 2 and 3, proposed weight `simple` (use the app normally with the AirPods for a day, then grep two event actions; no staged repro). Needs the owner's approval before the row moves to `owner-verification.md`. Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: `micCaptureAbandoned` is cleared only by start(), so after a recovery that reports resumed the watchdog and wake re-check stay shut for the rest of the recording; clear it on success (F027). **Amendment shipped 2026-11-17:** `recoverCapture`'s success tail clears the flag and logs `mic_capture_unabandoned` with the abandon reason; 1 test, red without the fix. The owner-owed walkthrough above is unchanged. |
| [REC16](./tasks/REC16.md) | A recovered engine stops hearing device changes, and a capture gap spanning system sleep is padded with the wrong clock | Recording | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-21 from the capture-health sweep run during the END10 and END11 investigation, not from an owner report. Three defects on `MeetingRecorder`'s mid-recording paths, filed together because they share one seam and one fixture. **One, a regression [REC15](./tasks/REC15.md) introduced 2026-08-19:** `armConfigurationChangeObserver()` registers with `object: engine` and is called from exactly one place inside `start()`, while REC15's new `recoverCapture` does `engine = AVAudioEngine()` mid-recording without re-arming it, so from the first recovery onward the observer watches an object nothing posts to and the device-swap failure it exists to catch runs unhandled for the rest of the recording. The other two replacement sites are harmless by position (one precedes the arm, one is the failed-start unwind). **Two, the two capture channels use different clocks:** the system-audio gap is measured with `ProcessInfo.processInfo.systemUptime`, which does not advance during system sleep, while the mic gap uses `Date()`. Measured 2026-08-17: `system_capture_lost` 17:45:14, `did_wake_while_recording` and `system_capture_resumed` 17:54:31, a 557.0 s wallclock gap padded with `gap_sec: 106.819` / `pad_frames: 5127309` and `pad_clamped: false`, so **450.2 s of padding is silently missing** and every system-channel sample after the wake is stamped seven and a half minutes early against the mic channel; the control case with no sleep in it (2026-08-19, 7 s gap, `gap_sec: 7.679`) shows the arithmetic is otherwise sound. Same wall-versus-monotonic class PIPE10 fixed on the summarize timeout. **Three, an unrecoverable configuration change never stops the recording:** every `configuration_change_unrecoverable` path ends in `reportRecoveryFailure()`, which notifies once and returns, so the recording runs to its normal end writing a dead mic channel (13 occurrences against 5 `configuration_change_recovered`), which satisfies "the recording dropped" as well as an early end does. Scope: re-arm the observer from a single `replaceEngine()` helper so a fourth site cannot reintroduce it, measure the system gap on the wall clock and re-check the pad cap that now starts mattering, and make an unrecoverable mic failure persist on the HUD rather than fire once. **P2 by the REC10 precedent, but defect two is arguably P1** since it corrupts the timeline rather than losing a rare path, and the owner may re-band. Explicitly not REC15's own subject (the 2026-08-19 raises at 10:46 and 11:08 predate that fix landing at 12:01:56 and are evidence for it), not the one-recovery-at-a-time latch (deliberate and commented), not the stall watchdog's 2.5 s detection latency, and not REC8's assertion scope. Spec in [`tasks/REC16.md`](./tasks/REC16.md). **Shipped 2026-08-22.** All 3 items landed. Item 1: every engine swap now goes through `replaceEngine(reason:)`, which re-arms the configuration-change observer, closing the regression REC15's own fix introduced; the fence counts bare `engine = AVAudioEngine()` assignments and expects exactly two. Item 2: the system-outage span moved to the wall clock, and `maxPadSeconds` moved 300 to 900 with it, re-derived rather than nudged, because measuring truthfully is what makes that cap bind at all (at 300 the 2026-08-17 case would have clamped and stayed misaligned by 257 s). The field corpus turned out larger than the spec knew: `system_capture_lost` has fired 4 times, not 0 as `ARCHITECTURE.md` claimed, with measured outages of 7.68 s, 7.83 s and 557.0 s, and that stale sentence is corrected in this commit. Item 3: a lost mic now shows a persistent HUD state for the rest of the recording, cleared on `dismiss()` so it cannot leak into the next meeting. **A deviation worth challenging:** the spec pointed at the degraded banner, and this uses the state label instead, because that banner carries a 'Retry system audio' action and an abandoned mic has no retry to offer. +10 tests, all 3 items measured red by reverting each in turn. **What is not claimed:** no runtime test drives a real engine replacement or a real outage (both need a live engine on real hardware, REC12's reason), so item 1's re-arm and item 2's clock are pinned by source fences plus the notification-binding property, not by execution. swift build + release clean, 2912 swift tests green, truth fences green. `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md`, `docs/GUIDE.md`, `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` and one `daemon/CLAUDE.md` rule updated; the JSX kit is untouched (best-effort per DSN30) and needs no geometry change, since `pillWidth` was already sized for the longer 'Off the record'. **Remainder is owner-only:** walkthrough steps 1 and 2, proposed weight complex (step 1 needs a working day's dwell), though step 2 is a standalone 3-minute lid-close check for item 2 alone. Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: the mic gap pad is unclamped, one buffer allocated on main and charged in full to the 512 MiB backlog cap (F028), pads from detection rather than the last buffer (F029, field: 6 to 16 ms pads while the mic ended 0.6 to 1.9 s short), skips the loudest-channel rule after a converter recovery (F156), and notifies per recovery (11 for 6 episodes); clamp and chunk, pad from the last buffer, collapse before converting, one notification per episode. **Amendment shipped 2026-11-17:** clamped, chunked pad through one helper both channels share (`pad_clamped`), gap from the last mic buffer or the previous pad's end, collapse-then-convert with the converter only on a rate change, one notification per episode; 24 tests. |

| [UX56](./tasks/UX56.md) | Preferences opens behind other windows | UX | partial | P2 | Shipped 2026-08-04; `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2247 tests 0 failures with 4 new (`WindowActivationManagerTests` 12 -> 16), truth fences clean, no pipeline change. **The regression question the spec put first is answered: yes, UX39.** `git log` on `PreferencesWindow.swift` names `70ea61e` as the last commit to touch either open branch, and its diff is the whole answer: before it both ran `makeKeyAndOrderFront` plus `NSApp.activate(ignoringOtherApps: true)` **synchronously** in the menu item's own call, so `PreferencesWindow` decides nothing about fronting any more and the fix went into the manager's sequence. **One correction to the spec's reasoning, which moves which case is broken:** the deferral does not become pointless "on second and later opens", because the last close drops the policy back to `.accessory`, so every status-bar open is a first-window open, raise and all. The already-`.regular` case is Preferences opened **while another Dock window is up**, a well-travelled path: the Library toolbar's gear, `⌘,` through UX48's main menu (which exists only while a Dock window is up), the workflow sheet's Vocabulary cross-link, and the permission deeplinks. It also has independent corroboration, since UX48's pickup measured `292dfe49`'s zero-opacity `⌘,` button firing, so `⌘,` over the Library was opening Preferences on 2026-07-31 and the owner reading that as nothing happening is very likely this same report seen twice. **The fix** narrows UX39's deferral from unconditional to "wait exactly while a policy raise is in flight", carried by a `policyRaiseInFlight` flag rather than by the first-window test, because two windows opened in one turn are one transition and the second must wait with the first or it activates against a still-accessory app and lands underneath the one opened before it; both branches run one shared closure so they cannot drift. UX39's guarantee holds by construction: nothing activates before a raise has landed. What could not be measured here is the deciding fact, whether macOS grants the deferred `NSApp.activate()`, since that turns on activation eligibility a CLI-launched probe never holds, so a headless probe would be declined either way and prove nothing. **Owner-owed:** the walkthrough at the end of the spec, re-sequenced because the filed steps 3 and 4 exercise only the first-window path, which this fix leaves alone and which would have passed before and after. Its load-bearing step is the new step 3 (`⌘,` or the toolbar gear over an open Library), with step 5 as the honesty check: if Preferences still opens behind on the status-bar path, the fix missed the reported case and the task comes back open. **Walkthrough run 2026-08-04. The fix works on the path it targeted and the task stays open on its own step 5, which is the honest outcome rather than a rounded-up one.** The owner found the discriminator: with another app explicitly focused and the Library up, opening Preferences from the toolbar gear or Cmd+, brings it to the front **and to the head of the Cmd+Tab stack**, which is the already-`.regular` case this task narrowed the deferral for. Closing the Library and opening Preferences from the status-bar dropdown with nothing else up still opens **behind the frontmost app**, and step 5 pre-registered that as the task coming back open. That path is untouched by this commit and byte-identical before and after it (`shown.count == 1` raises the policy, so `policyRaiseInFlight` is true and the fronting still waits a run-loop turn), so this is a residual rather than a regression. **The new finding, and the reason the residual is worth more than a repeat report:** on that same deferred path the owner observed MeetingPipe at the **tail** of the Cmd+Tab stack, which is precisely what `WindowActivationManager.swift:106` says the deferral exists to prevent. So on the one path that still defers, the deferral is paying its cost and not buying the MRU position it was built to buy, which is evidence against the deferral itself rather than for a wider one. **Second pass 2026-08-04, answering exactly that: the wait is gone and UX39's guarantee is kept as a retry.** `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2321 tests 0 failures (`WindowActivationManagerTests` 16 -> 17), truth fences clean, no pipeline change. Two readings of the finding were available, that the wait is too short or that it is the wrong mechanism, and only the second has anything behind it: there is no length at which giving the other app a turn to reclaim focus starts helping this app claim it, and the owner's own A/B is that the path with no wait came up in front and at the head while the path with one came up behind and at the tail. So `showWindow` fronts and activates **every** window in the turn that asked for it, the turn the user's click is still in, and a policy raise queues exactly **one** `activate` for the turn after, when `setActivationPolicy` has certainly landed: a no-op if the first attempt was granted, and the promotion UX39 wanted if it was evaluated against the still-accessory app. The retry is queued at the raise rather than per window, so two windows opened in one turn front in ask order with the second on top and share it, which is what the first pass's flag existed to protect and is now true by construction; `policyRaiseInFlight` is gone and `showWindow` has no branch left. **This is not the naive revert the spec rules out**, and `70ea61e` is what shows it: pre-UX39 each call site ran `makeKeyAndOrderFront` plus `activate` **and then** handed over to a manager that raised the policy and activated again, so the app asked to be frontmost before it had asked to be regular. UX39 bundled the ordering fix with the deferral and only the bundle was verified; the ordering half is the half with a mechanism behind it and is untouched, so raise-then-activate-then-retry is a third position that has never shipped. The already-passing toolbar-gear and Cmd+, path is byte-identical, since a show with no raise pending took the same three statements through the old `guard`. **Still owner-owed, and the walkthrough is down to three owner steps.** The tests pin call *order* through the injected `Effects`, which is all that seam can see; the deciding fact is whether macOS **grants** the activation, which turns on eligibility held from the user's click on the status item, so a unit test cannot reach it, a CLI probe never holds it, and a synthetic click confers none and moves frontmost itself. Step 2 (Preferences from the status-bar dropdown with another app frontmost) is the whole task; step 3 is its MRU half, where a tail means the retry buys nothing either; step 4 is the UX39 guard on the Library, the one thing this change could plausibly break. **Amended 2026-08-10 with an owner report that the Library shows the symptom too, plus a discriminator that is new to this spec.** The Library half answers the question this spec's Context put in the open (it noted the Library was *not* reported with the symptom, "which is itself a clue"): the answer is that nothing differs, both windows go through the same `showWindow` and both can land behind, which removes the last reason to look for a Preferences-side cause. **Date it before acting on it:** the owner's `app_rebuild` events that day are 09:52, 10:25, 13:26, 15:25 and 22:06 local, and this observation sits above the 10:37 screenshots in the notes, so it was made on a build carrying the 01:41 first pass but **not** the 18:27 second pass that rewrote this path. It does establish that walkthrough step 4 has failed at least once on a first-pass build. The discriminator is the part worth more than a repeat report, and it is a variable absent from this spec's reasoning: focus the Teams **call** window and the Library fronts correctly, focus the Teams **main** window and it lands behind. Hypothesis to test rather than assert, in this repo's own terms: MIC10's live AX dump makes the in-call surface a floating `AXSystemDialog` panel, and a panel is not the activation holder an ordinary window is, so "opens properly" may be the absence of a competitor rather than a success. That gives a within-case control (same app, same moment, two window kinds, opposite outcomes) which could separate "macOS declined" from "the ordering is wrong" without a probe that can hold eligibility. Walkthrough steps 4 and 5 now cover both. |
| [UX69](./tasks/UX69.md) | A running job cannot be cancelled for its first minute | UX | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-04 from UX50's second pass, measured on the owner's own run of `20260716-163031`: Reprocess clicked 13:03:39.998Z, first pipeline heartbeat 13:04:38.629Z, so the row offers no Cancel for **58.6 s**. **Both legs shipped 2026-08-18; the escape hatch was investigated and not taken. The `partial` is the owner walkthrough alone, and no dev remainder is owed.** Leg (a): `SinkDispatcher.invokePipeline` publishes the row's entry when it spawns, which is the same main queue turn `runMP` puts that spawn in `cancelActiveRun`'s slot, so the control never precedes the thing it signals. Leg (b): `runDaemonTranscription` runs in a held `transcriptionTask`, `cancelActiveJob` signals it and `cancelActiveRun` unconditionally (each a no-op for the half that is not live, so neither the click nor the queue has to know which one is), and a cancelled pass lands exactly as UX50's run-all cancel does, `.interrupted` with `cancelledReason`, retryable, no `engine_failed`, no `pipeline_failed`, no banner, no notification. **`PipelineLauncher.isCancellation` is the one predicate for both**, since a click reports as `LaunchError.cancelled` from a killed spawn and as a `CancellationError` from a cancelled Task; `SinkDispatcher.stage(for:)` now asks it first rather than keeping a second copy of that knowledge. **The FluidAudio finding, which is why leg (b) was shippable:** its Parakeet path is already cooperatively cancellable, `ChunkProcessor` calling `Task.checkCancellation()` once per chunk and `AsrManager+Pipeline` again before every CoreML prediction, so a click during ASR (the phase that scales with meeting length and the one the walkthrough's "click immediately" lands in) is honoured inside a chunk. `DiarizerManager.performCompleteDiarization` is synchronous and declares no checkpoint, so a click landing inside diarization is honoured when it returns; that is the one stated bound, it is upstream's rather than this repo's, and it was not closed by driving the diarizer chunk by chunk here because that re-implements its speaker tracking and moves output DIAR2 tuned against a hand-labelled corpus (DIAR4's migration, not a cancel). The Cancel is therefore never a dead control, which is the fence the spec set. **The marker-on-every-exit trap was real and is proven rather than asserted:** a cancelled pass has two exits and only one throws, so `runDaemonTranscription` carries its own `try Task.checkCancellation()` after the runner returns; removing that one line and re-running turns the test red with a spawned pipeline and a **nil** sidecar stage, which is the empty-recording shape (a row stuck in Processing until the 2 h age fallback renames it `Failed` with no reason). Each of the four production lines was removed and re-run red before it went in. A `.retranscribe` deliberately publishes no entry and so offers no Cancel: it runs from a surface showing its own progress (ASR3). Row copy: the pass reads **Transcribing** and the spawned-but-silent subprocess reads **Processing**, both without a clock, because a timer frozen at 0:00 for a minute reads as a stuck row. Verified: `swift build` clean, `swift test` 0 failures with **11 new**, `truth_fences.py both` green, ruff / pyright / pytest green (no pipeline change). No new event action, config key, or sidecar field. Docs: README's processing paragraph, `docs/GUIDE.md`'s Stopping recipe and its Interrupted paragraph, ARCHITECTURE's `SinkDispatcher` and transcription bullets, `daemon/CLAUDE.md`'s cancellation rule (broadened byte-neutrally, the file is at its CI5 cap) and its gotchas section. **Remainder: the owner walkthrough at the end of [`tasks/UX69.md`](./tasks/UX69.md)**, steps 2 to 5, whose load-bearing step is 3, clicking Cancel while the row still says Transcribing. The row stays here rather than moving to [`owner-verification.md`](owner-verification.md): that move needs the owner's approval and a recorded weight, and neither exists yet. Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: Cancel sets no Cancelling state, so the row keeps its spinner and an enabled Cancel until the work stops; a small dev leg to show Cancelling at the click (P029), which needs the owner's agreement since it reopens a dev remainder. |
| [UX75](./tasks/UX75.md) | Digest action groups after the first reserve their rows' height and draw nothing | UX | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-10 from the owner's 2026-08-04 notes and two screenshots: the digest's Action items renders `GENERAL` with its five rows, then `PERSONAL`, a meeting-titled group, and `CLIENT WORK` each followed by hundreds of points of blank, while the `Action scope` footer on the same screen says "Personal: 5 of 102 shown" and "Client work: 5 of 9 shown". **The data is complete, so nothing upstream needs looking at**: `digest-20260803.summary.md` carries all 16 checkbox lines and `digest-20260803.summary.json` carries all 16 `actions` with their `group` fields populated. The defect is in the shared renderer that `DigestReaderView` delegates to. `SummaryRenderedView.swift:80-101` builds the list as a `LazyVStack` whose single outer `ForEach` emits a header `Text` plus a **nested** `ForEach` per run, so the lazy container's direct children are compound subtrees rather than a flat row sequence; the group headers draw, which proves the runs exist, and the blank space scales with each group's row count, which is laid-out-but-not-drawn. `actionRuns` itself was read and is correct, including the index mapping UX26's toggle writes through. Scope: flatten the runs into one homogeneous row sequence (`.groupHeader` / `.action`) behind a single `ForEach`, preserving that index mapping and the nil-group flat case, and decide at pickup whether AI10's bounded list needs a lazy container at all. The rendered result is owner-owed; unit tests can pin the flattening but not the pixels. **Shipped 2026-08-18; `partial` on the owner walkthrough alone, and no dev remainder is owed.** The list is now built from `SummaryRenderedView.actionListRows`, one flat homogeneous sequence of `SummaryRenderedView.ActionListRow` (`.groupHeader(name:runStart:)` / `.action`), behind a single `ForEach`. The nested `ForEach` is gone, so the `LazyVStack` is handed one child per row instead of one compound subtree per run. **`actionRuns` is untouched and stays the source of truth**: the flatten carries `ActionItemRow.Action` whole rather than re-spelling its fields, so UX26's write-back index cannot drift on the way through, and the existing `testActionRowsCarryTheirIndexIntoTheSummary` passes unmodified. Identities are the position a row already owns in `summary.actions` for an action, and the index of the first row it heads for a heading, named by position rather than by group text because `actionRuns` splits into *runs* and not buckets: a writer emitting `A, B, A` legitimately produces two headings reading `A`, and the text alone would collide in a `ForEach`. **The lazy container stays, decided rather than defaulted.** The comment above it records a real scroll freeze; the view is shared with the meeting Summary tab and with the A16 compare pane, which stacks two of them; `ActionItemRow.metadataBand`'s own doc justifies its `ViewThatFits` by these rows sitting in a `LazyVStack` inside a `ScrollView`, so dropping the container would falsify a documented premise one screen away; and a flat sequence is exactly the shape a lazy container realizes correctly, so keeping it costs nothing now that the nesting is gone. **The geometry is preserved by construction rather than re-tuned**: same container spacing (`MPSpace.s2`), same modifier chain on both row kinds, including the heading's own `.padding(.top, MPSpace.s2)`, which is what puts more air above a heading than between a heading and the first row under it. No spacing, font or colour value was changed. 7 tests added to `ActionResolveTests`, each run against a deliberately reintroduced defect first. Remainder: the owner walkthrough in the spec, which is the only thing that can show the rows actually draw. |
| [UX76](./tasks/UX76.md) | Three accreted captions on the workflow rail row truncate the workflow name itself | UX | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-10 from the owner's 2026-08-04 notes and screenshot: the rail reads `Pers… · defa… · no c…` on their main workflow, so "none of the words properly read" and the row identifies nothing. `WorkflowScopeRow` (`LibrarySidebar.swift:472-513`) puts five things in one `HStack` under a single `.lineLimit(1)`: the emoji, the name, `· default`, WF9's duplicate-name caption, and FEAT11's `· no context` marker. Each caption arrived with its own task and each is individually defensible; nobody owned the **total width budget**, so in a fixed-width rail all five compete and the name has no priority over the decorations describing it. The other rows corroborate the mechanism exactly: `General` carries no captions and reads fine, `Client work` carries one and keeps its name, `Personal` carries two and loses everything. The row degrades backwards, since the name is what the user clicks and the captions are footnotes about it. Scope is a layout **policy** decision, not tuning (the smallest change that keeps five items on one line reproduces this at a slightly narrower rail): weigh layout priority, demoting the two boolean markers to glyphs with their existing `.help` text, or a second line. Constraints: all three facts stay reachable with their tooltips and accessibility labels, DSN3's 4.5:1 floor holds (`fgSubtle` clears it, `fgFaint` does not), no new `.system(size:)` literals, and FEAT11's per-Text tooltip must keep not fighting WF9's row-level one. Explicitly not removing a caption, not widening the rail, and not WF9's underlying duplicate-name situation. **Engineering shipped 2026-08-19; the remainder is the owner walkthrough only.** The policy chosen is the spec's second and first directions together, because either alone postpones the defect: the three footnotes are booleans, so each is now one `fgSubtle` glyph instead of a phrase, and the name carries `.layoutPriority(1)` so whatever is still tight truncates footnotes first and the name last. Composition moved into a pure `WorkflowRailRow.decide` in `LibrarySidebar.swift`, which is what makes the reported defect assertable at all (SwiftUI offers no runtime read of what a layout truncated) and what forces a fourth caption to come through one place rather than being appended to the `HStack` the way the first three were. **No fact was dropped to make room:** each marker keeps its VoiceOver label and its tooltip, and WF9's nil `help` is the deliberate layering (its sentence stays on the row so it does not fight FEAT11's element-level one) rather than an omission, pinned by its own test. **The neighbours were measured rather than eyeballed, and one had a different defect:** the smart-folder row has no competition (built-in short titles, one text against a fixed count), but `SavedSearchScopeRow` was missing `.lineLimit(1)` entirely, so a long user-typed saved-search name grew the row to two or three lines instead of truncating like every neighbour; fixed here since it is the same row family. Also tokenised the emoji's raw `.system(size: 11)` to `.mpTextXS`, the identical 11pt, since the DSN3 guard rejects raw literals on changed lines. 10 tests added; `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2756 tests 0 failures, truth fences clean, DSN3 and dash guards clean, no pipeline change. Docs updated in the same commit: three `README.md` claims, one in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, one in `docs/GUIDE.md`, all of which described the captions as words. `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` gained the reusable lesson (a caption added to a fixed-width row is a width decision), which is the part worth more than the fix. The `SummaryLibrary.jsx` mock **does** model this row, unlike the HUD's, so it is genuinely drifted now; re-mocking needs glyph shapes chosen against the running build, which is the owner's judgement and DSN30's scope, so the kit README carries a precise lag note instead. **Owner-owed:** the walkthrough, proposed weight `simple` (open the Library and read the rail; no recording, no call, no waiting). Needs the owner's approval before the row moves to `owner-verification.md`. |
| [UX77](./tasks/UX77.md) | The toolbar calls queued work "Processing N" while the row calls the same job "Queued" | UX | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-10 from the owner's 2026-08-04 notes and a two-box screenshot: the Library toolbar pill reads **Processing 1** while the meeting's inline row reads **Queued: Re-transcribe**, one job with two contradictory nouns on one screen. The pill is fed the **queue depth**, not a count of running work (`Coordinator.swift:326` `onQueueDepth` to `StatusBarController.setProcessingCount` to `LibraryToolbar.swift:130`), and the same number drives the menu-bar title clause and both `StatusBarModel` menu variants, so the mislabel is on four surfaces. The per-meeting row is the correct half: `ProcessingPresentation.label` (`LibraryProcessing.swift:44-56`) deliberately distinguishes `.active` (stage plus elapsed) from `.queued` ("Queued: ..."). In the screenshot the queue held exactly one not-yet-started item, so "Processing 1" was describing zero running jobs. Scope is mostly a copy decision: either one neutral noun for the union (`In queue 3`) or a split that matches the row vocabulary (`Processing 1 · 2 queued`, which costs a second value through the `onQueueDepth` seam), derived once so the four surfaces cannot drift. Preserve DSN7's one-clause menu-bar title and `ProcessingTracker`'s separate-object optimisation. Explicitly not `LibraryProcessing`, not the queue itself, not the "Stopping…" pill; UX69 and the regenerate-bypasses-the-queue behaviour are adjacent and stay separate. **Engineering shipped 2026-08-19; the remainder is the owner walkthrough only.** The discriminator already existed one line from the seam (`activeJob != nil` in `SinkDispatcher`) and had simply never been published, so `onQueueDepthChanged: ((Int) -> Void)` became `onQueueActivityChanged: ((QueueActivity) -> Void)` carrying `depth` plus `isRunning`, and all three aggregate surfaces now take their word from one `QueueActivityPresentation.noun`. **A third option was taken over the spec's two.** A fixed neutral noun (`In queue 3`) loses the fact that something is running and drops PRODUCT.md's menu-bar vocabulary; the split (`Processing 1 · 2 queued`) is richer but is two clauses, and DSN7 gives the title exactly one. So the noun describes the head and the number counts the whole queue, which is how a person says it out loud: `Queued 1` before anything starts, `Processing 1` once it does. The reported case is fixed exactly, since the screenshot's queue had not started. **Note for walkthrough step 4:** with one running behind one waiting this reads `Processing 2`, so the noun describes the head rather than counting only running work; if that reads wrong to you the split is the fallback and would need DSN7 revisited. `ProcessingTracker` publishes one `QueueActivity` rather than two properties, per the spec's separate-object note. 10 tests added (9 new `QueueActivityPresentationTests` covering the reported case, the one-derivation fence across all four call sites, DSN7's single clause, and the row staying untouched; 1 on the seam), plus 3 existing suites updated to the widened seam. `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2766 tests 0 failures, truth fences clean, no pipeline change. Docs: `ARCHITECTURE.md` (the seam's shape), `PRODUCT.md` (its menu-bar literal-state list gains `Queued`), and `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` for the reusable lesson, that an aggregate label needs the distinction the per-item surface already makes. `docs/GUIDE.md` was read per the spec and needs no edit: it names the per-row status pill, which is a different surface and unchanged. **Measured while filing that rule: `daemon/CLAUDE.md` is genuinely full**, 58 bytes under CI5's 24,000-byte whole-file backstop, so the one-line rule could not be filed beside its `SummarizationEngine` and `PublishLanding` siblings and lives only in `gotchas.md`. **Owner-owed:** the walkthrough, proposed weight `simple` (queue two re-transcribes from the Library and read three surfaces; no live call). Needs the owner's approval before the row moves to `owner-verification.md`. **Cross-referenced from PIPE18 2026-08-19, per that task's instruction to link the burst here once this row was picked up:** the 2026-08-14 screenshot showing `Processing 7` over a queue of one active job and six waiting is a sharper instance of this defect than the one it was filed on, and reads correctly under the fix as `Processing 7` only while the head is running. |
| [UX78](./tasks/UX78.md) | Audio keeps playing after leaving the Transcript or Audio tab, with no control on Summary | UX | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-10 from the owner's 2026-08-04 notes: "when playing transcript, and switching to summary, it would be nice to have the possibility to stop playback, but I have no controls, and playback still running." `AudioPlaybackController` is a `@StateObject` on `MeetingDetailView.swift:49`, so it outlives a tab switch, which is correct ownership and is what lets a summary citation jump into the transcript and keep its position. The gap is that every transport control lives inside `AudioTab` (and the per-line play affordances inside `TranscriptTab`), while `tabButton` (`:174`) only writes `selectedTab` and nothing consults `playback`, so selecting Summary removes the whole surface while the audio continues; `TranscriptTab.onDisappear` removes its right-click monitor and nothing else. The two existing `pause()` calls show this is an omission rather than a policy: both are local (a sheet opening at `TranscriptTab.swift:440`, a right-click at `:459`), so the code already knows playback must yield when attention moves and handles two narrow cases while the widest one is unhandled. With no visible control the only exits are switching back or closing the window. Scope: a persistent mini transport in the detail header while `isPlaying` (answers the ask literally, keeps continuity; the controller already publishes `isPlaying` / `currentTime` / `duration` and has `togglePlayPause`) or a pause on leaving the audio-bearing tabs (two lines, no chrome, but discards an intentional listen-while-reading). Say which and why. Do not move ownership, and check that selecting a different meeting stops the previous one's audio, which was not verified at filing. **Engineering shipped 2026-08-19; the remainder is the owner walkthrough only.** Option A, the header transport, which is what the owner asked for ("the possibility to stop playback", a control rather than silence). Option B was deliberately NOT taken: an auto-pause on leaving the tab would silently discard an intentional listen-while-reading and still leaves the user without a control, so the header now carries play/pause plus the position on every tab and nothing pauses on a tab switch. `DetailTransport.decide` is the pure rule, and two of its three clauses are not about playing. **A paused play head still shows the control**, or the press that stops the audio is the press that hides the way to resume it, which is why the test is not a bare `isPlaying`. **And the loaded file has to be this meeting's:** the detail view has no `.id(meeting.stem)`, so SwiftUI reuses it and the player survives a selection change with the previous meeting's file still loaded, while only the two audio-bearing tabs call `load`. **The spec asked for that case to be checked before and after, and it was broken:** switching meetings from Summary replaced nothing, so the meeting you just left kept playing under the new title. Fixed here as the spec directs, by pausing in the stem-change hook, with the URL check making a stale transport unreachable rather than unlikely. Ownership was not moved, and the citation jump is fenced (its seek must still be raised before the tab switch, and it must not pause). 11 tests added (8 pure over `decide`, 3 source fences for the SwiftUI wiring, which has no runtime read). `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2777 tests 0 failures, truth fences, DSN3 and dash guards clean, no pipeline change. Docs: `README.md` and `docs/GUIDE.md` (its shortcut table implied the only way to stop playback was those two tabs), `ARCHITECTURE.md`, and `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` for the reusable lesson, that a shared controller needs a control outliving the surfaces sharing it, with the tell being local pauses accumulating in a child. The `SummaryLibrary.jsx` mock models this header and now lags it, so the kit README carries a note; rendering is owner-owed there as here. **Owner-owed:** the walkthrough, proposed weight `simple` (open a meeting, play, switch tabs; no live call). Needs the owner's approval before the row moves to `owner-verification.md`. Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: F069, a meeting with no audio still drives the previous meeting's loaded file from the Transcript bar, row click and Audio footer, so an unload and a loaded-URL guard on every surface precede the walkthrough. |
| [UX79](./tasks/UX79.md) | The menu reads "MeetingPipe: Idle" and offers an inert Start Recording for the whole of a stop | UX | partial | P2 | Filed 2026-08-18 from ARCH6's second finding, which it recorded rather than fixed because the fix changes what the menu says **and shows** mid-stop and ARCH6's acceptance forbade user-visible change. `StatusBarController.setStopping` sets `titleState = .stopping` and then calls `rebuildMenu(state: .idle)`, so the menu-bar title reads "Stopping…" while the menu header one click away reads "MeetingPipe: Idle", and `headerLabel`'s `.stopping` branch stays test-only. ARCH6 already corrected T3's archive claim that deriving the header "makes the author's own case live"; it does not. **Measured for this filing and not previously recorded**: the Start Recording row the `.idle` menu carries through the flush is **inert**, since `Coordinator.menuStart()` is `session.toggleManual()` whose `.stopping` case is a bare `break` ("a new start would race the await"), while `StatusBarModel.rows(_:)`'s own comment already says "`.stopping` is a genuine no-op mid-flush". So the archive's stated cost of fixing this, losing the Start row, is the correct behaviour rather than a reason not to. **And the window is not always brief**: it runs from `setStopping` to `setIdle` across the merge, and REC13's watchdog comment records 52 minutes in `.stopping` on 2026-08-14. Scope: decide whether the header and its row set follow the stopping state (three routes costed in the spec, none pre-selected), pin it with a controller test so a later caller cannot pass `.idle` again, and correct T3's body in `q6-final.md`. Route choice is owner-gated: a session cannot screenshot a menu-bar menu. Spec in [`tasks/UX79.md`](./tasks/UX79.md). **Engineering shipped 2026-08-19, route A, the walkthrough's stated default.** The whole fix is one line: `setStopping` hands `rebuildMenu(state: titleState)` instead of a literal `.idle`. The header now reads `MeetingPipe: Stopping…` and agrees with the title, `headerLabel`'s `.stopping` branch is reached in production rather than only from tests, and `rows(_:)` takes its `default: break`, so the flush offers no transport row at all, which is what that switch's own comment already said it wanted. **The spec's question about `lastMenuState` is answered: yes, route A makes the two identical in every path.** Every setter now writes `titleState = X` and hands `rebuildMenu` the same X; `setStopping` was the only divergence. The collapse is still not taken here, because the three permission-driven rebuilds pass `lastMenuState` and that is a refactor with its own blast radius; the field's doc now says so rather than repeating the old justification. **The fence is on the call site, not just the output**, which is the regression that actually recurs: the `.idle` was passed deliberately three times over, and every model-level test here would stay green if a fourth caller restored it. That fence caught its own weakness on the first run, since the explanatory comment pushed the call past a byte-count window, so it is scoped to the function body instead. 7 tests added. `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2822 tests 0 failures, truth fences and dash guard clean, no pipeline change. **T3's body in `q6-final.md` is corrected as the acceptance required**, and further than it asked: ARCH6's finding was also moved into past tense, because "`setStopping` still passes `.idle`" and "is not true now" had become present-tense false statements the moment this landed, which is the doc-truth defect this task warns about. `docs/GUIDE.md` gained a line on what the menu shows mid-stop; `README.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` checked and neither describes the menu's transport rows. `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` carries the reusable lesson, that a documented divergence is still a divergence, with the point that three revisions re-justified the split rather than questioning it. **Owner-owed:** walkthrough step 3, proposed weight `simple` (start a manual recording, stop it, open the menu while the title reads Stopping; no live call). One treatment per case, as the spec asked for. **Fold the fact in** for "Moving to a new Mac" (a fresh Mac happens before the app exists there, so the restore runs from the Terminal and the keys never come across either way) and for "Anything else" (the README pointer replaced by the two failure families that actually reach a user, a destination refusing and an engine refusing, both ending at a control rather than a file). **Drop the pointer** for the README-as-the-reference sentence and the glossary. **Mark it repo-only** for the contents list, since the section rail is the app's copy of it and two contents lists with one of them dead text is worse than one, plus a preamble carrying the rule for the next editor. `<!-- repo-only -->` blocks are dropped by the parser and shown by every markdown renderer, which is what lets the single copy DOC11 chose keep serving both readers. **`GuideDocument.flatten` was inverted from a shape blocklist to an allowlist** (`canFollow`, over `http` / `https` / `mailto`): the shapes that fail are open-ended and the ones that work are a closed set, and rejecting shapes is precisely how DOC11 caught `#anchor` and left these five standing beside the code that flattened it. Two fences, neither redundant: one walks the parsed blocks for a surviving target containing `../` or ending in `.md` (the property the reader experiences), the other walks the source outside the markers for the same shapes (a repo path parked inside a repo-only block would pass the first and still sit in the file as the shape the next edit copies). **Decision not in the spec, named for challenge:** no repo path survives anywhere in the file, including inside repo-only blocks, so the asides name `docs/SETUP.md` and `README.md` as backticked filenames rather than links; the cost is that a GitHub reader gets a name instead of a click, and the benefit is that the fence is checkable over the raw source too. `GuideDocument.missingCopy` is the one deliberate exception and the reasoning is recorded so it does not get "fixed": it is the only guide state with no control behind it, re-running the installer is the entire fix, and what went was the sentence naming which file it ships as. **Design half, worked against `design/README.md` and `PRODUCT.md` rather than taste.** Three things carried the owner's "looks ugly like it come from 90s". The rail had no selected state at all, where every other rail in the app marks its row with `mpSelectionWash` under an uppercase eyebrow; body copy sat at `fgMuted`, the caption colour, so a whole document read as secondary text; and every heading level got identical space above it while ten `---` rules each landed a hairline a few points above a section heading. Now: rail at the Library's 220pt with a Contents eyebrow, teal wash on the section being read (a pure `GuideDocument.currentSection` in the `decide()` idiom over probes on the ten level-2 headings, so it is unit-tested rather than buried in a view body), `mpOverlayHover` under the pointer, `MPRadius.sm` rows, and an `.isSelected` trait because the wash is colour-only otherwise. Body copy at `fg`, heading leads stepped 0 / s8 / s5, section rules collapsed into the heading so whitespace separates (the blank paper is the texture), bullet glyph and 16pt gutter matched to `SummaryRenderedView`, table header on a `bgSunk` band clipped inside the radius, and the window widened to 1000x700 so the column actually reaches `SummaryLayout.readingMeasure` (220 + 1 + 720 + 40 = 981; at DOC11's 940 every paragraph wrapped early). Typography moved from the fixed ramp to **Dynamic Type**, which is both a `PRODUCT.md` a11y-floor item (it names markdown rendering explicitly) and a sibling-matching one (transcript lines and summary bullets are `.font(.body)`), chosen so the default sizes are unchanged: macOS resolves `.title` to 22 like `MPType.textXL`, `.title2` to 17, `.title3` to 15, `.body` to 13, `.callout` to 12. The rail stays fixed-size because it is chrome. No new `.system(size:` literals, no `fgFaint`, contrast unchanged. **Flagged and deliberately not decided:** the guide's H1 reads "Using meeting-pipe" under a titlebar reading "MeetingPipe Guide", and `PRODUCT.md` itself uses the repo spelling as the product name, so that is a naming decision across three documents rather than a repo-path cleanup; the owner declined to fold it into DOC13 on 2026-08-18. **Owner-owed, and this is the whole remainder:** *what to do* is `./scripts/rebuild.sh`, open the Guide from the menu bar and read it once as a user, then put it beside the Library and Preferences; *a pass* is the three checks in the walkthrough at the end of [`tasks/DOC13.md`](./tasks/DOC13.md), of which the load-bearing one is check 3, that the window reads as the same application as the two beside it; *the cost* is a few minutes, one rebuild, no live call and no waiting, which is what makes it `simple`; *why a harness cannot* is that checks 2 and 4 are now the two tests over `docs/GUIDE.md` and are green, while the visual one cannot be reached at all here, because the daemon is a menu-bar app that is not screenshotable in the harness and nobody in the shipping session ever saw the surface render. Tokens, idioms and sibling surfaces were matched rather than pixels; whether that lands is the owner's eye. |

The preview needed a contract, so `mp restore` grew `--dry-run --json [--out PATH]` (`plan_restore` -> a `RestorePlan` TypedDict: per-root destination, files landing, how many are new, how many already exist and get overwritten, whether the root is occupied at all, plus capped samples so the confirm can *name* the meeting that comes back). Plan and real run are one walk seen twice through a shared `_restorable_members`. **The real run passes `--force`, always**, because every in-app restore writes into live roots that a plain `mp restore` refuses, so the preview *is* the refusal it replaces. `RestoreFlow.confirm()` only acts from `.confirming`, so the real run is unreachable except through a shown plan; `StorageBusyGate` makes backup and restore mutually exclusive. 2052 daemon tests green (23 new), 1171 pipeline (14 new), ruff/pyright/truth fences/dash guard clean. **Owner-owed:** the walkthrough at the end of the spec. Its load-bearing step is step 6, reading the confirm before pressing it: that the preview names the files that were moved out, admits how many it overwrites, and can be cancelled with nothing written. It is the only guard in front of a `--force` overwrite of the library, so it stays owner-owed rather than done. Amended 2026-11-13 by the audit band: a finished restore never reloads the workflow, saved-search and workflow-correction stores, so the next edit writes the pre-restore copies back, and the retention sweep re-drops restored audio; unshipped follow-on, relaunch after a restore until it lands. |

| [UX45](./tasks/UX45.md) | Publish-target configuration in-app | UX | simple | P2 | Shipped 2026-08-03, every scope item landed; both suites green (2043 Swift from 2029, 1160 Python from 1157; ruff, pyright, truth fences, dash guard clean). **Preferences ▸ Integrations** now carries the three non-Notion destinations after the credential groups (Obsidian vault + notes subfolder + attach-audio + attachments subfolder, the Filesystem output folder, the LAN mount path + share label), each a path field beside a `Choose…` panel with a status pill, plus **Include the full transcript** in the Notion group and a **Model id** field in Pipeline whenever the backend picker says OpenAI, replacing the hand-edit instruction. Nine new `ConfigStore` bindings, all round-tripping with unmodelled siblings preserved (`obsidian.template_path` and `daily_note_backlink` stay config-only by design and are pinned as such). **The LAN sink crosses the sidecar contract end to end**: `WorkflowSink` has a fourth case, `WorkflowStore.decode` a fourth branch (before this it hit `default: continue`, so even a hand-written `type = "lan"` was dropped on load), and a fifth committed meta-contract fixture (`workflow-lan`) is asserted on both sides, so the `"lan"` literal the daemon writes is the one `publish_router._build_one` dispatches on. The readiness derivation is a pure `PublishTargets` shared by the Integrations pills and the editor's inline caption, so the two surfaces cannot disagree about whether a target is set up. **One correction found by writing the test**: `config.effective_sinks` preserves the LAN sink under zero egress on the grounds that an on-prem share is not egress, but every recorded meeting reaches the publishers through `mp.workflow.apply_overrides` first, whose belt-and-braces clamp is filesystem-only for regulated and NDA alike, so a LAN sink does **not** in practice publish under either. That divergence is now pinned by a test and the four places that claimed otherwise (README, `config.example.toml`, and two draft strings on the new surfaces) say the true thing. Also fixed in passing, since UX45 is what makes it false: README's Workflows section claimed per-workflow Obsidian vault routing that no field ever provided (DOC12 item 3), and `SETUP.md` step 8 sent a non-programmer to a config file for a vault path that now has a control. **Two decisions not in the spec**, both named for challenge: the new defaults stay inline in `ConfigStore` rather than moving to `ConfigDefaults`, because that file's own docstring reserves itself for knobs more than one Swift site reads and these have exactly one; and the three groups sit under the existing pane header with no "Publish targets" heading of their own, because DSN28 locks one type anchor per surface, so the pane's caption carries the framing instead. **Owner-owed:** the spec's eyeball, per the walkthrough in the task file. Rendering is the part a build and 2043 unit tests cannot reach, and this task adds three Preferences groups, a checkbox, and a caption. **Pre-existing drift noticed, not fixed here:** `design/ui_kits/macos_app/PreferencesWindow.jsx`'s Integrations recreation was already behind the shipped pane before this task (no OpenAI credential group from PROV1/UX31, and a raw "Database ID" field where UX22 ships the fetched picker). **A third way was recorded here and was already wrong when written:** the `secrets.env` caption had been fixed by this task's own commit `1a4ddb6`, which replaced it with the Keychain wording. DSN30 caught that on 2026-08-19 and closed the two real ones by re-mocking the pane. The publish-target groups and the transcript toggle were added to it so it reflects this change; the older gaps are left for DOC12's sweep rather than silently absorbed. **Walkthrough partly run 2026-08-04, stays owner-owed by the owner's choice.** The Integrations controls work: the owner configured publish targets through the new pane and switched their `Personal` workflow from the filesystem sink to Obsidian, disabling filesystem, so the first clause of the eyeball is exercised. An apparent defect raised during the pass ("the output folder is empty while I had meetings today") was **investigated and is not one**: the configured filesystem directory holds 366 files including four written today, and six of the seven meetings recorded on 2026-08-04 published `full`. The seventh, `20260804-133028`, correctly never reached publish and carries a `.empty.json` terminal marker with 0 segments and 0 characters, which is the no-speech skip path working as designed rather than a publish failure. The owner's own diagnosis was that they had pointed the **filesystem** sink at their Obsidian folder while reading the Obsidian one. Still owed: the owner's observation of the new Obsidian routing on future meetings, the NDA-workflow unconfigured-caption clause, and the transcript-toggle clause, which needs a Notion publish and is unexercisable while nothing classifies as `General`. |
| [UX73](./tasks/UX73.md) | The HUD flag-a-moment acknowledgement is a whole-panel opacity dip and nothing else | UX | simple | P2 | Filed 2026-08-10 from the owner's 2026-08-04 notes: "clicking on it gives no visual reaction except the fact that hood become semitransparent. Pretty weird feeling." Follow-on to UX46, not a regression: the report is exactly what the code does on purpose. `flagMoment` (`MeetingSessionController.swift:266`) calls `hud.blink()`, which animates the **whole panel's** `alphaValue` to 0.4 and back (`RecordingHUDWindow.swift:232`, "a single quiet opacity blink ... No persistent chrome"). Three things make that read as no reaction: it is a window-level gesture macOS also uses for losing focus, so it reads as the HUD reacting to the click rather than to the flag and the button itself never changes; it leaves nothing behind, so a user watching the call window cannot learn afterwards that the press registered; and it says nothing about what happened, since `flagMoment` computes both the elapsed offset and the marker index and sends **both to the log only** at `:277-282`. Nothing else surfaces them during the call either: `markerSeconds` is written by `MarkerFile.write` at stop time, so the first confirmation arrives after the meeting. Both entry points (button and right-click item) share one delegate hop, so one fix covers both. Scope: a legible acknowledgement local to the control, using the count and offset already in hand, weighed against PRODUCT.md's quiet register since this sits over a live call and possibly a screen share. Explicitly not the marker mechanism, not an undo or an in-HUD marker list, and not UX27's keyboard operability. Owner-owed by construction: the deliverable is what a control looks like when pressed. **Engineering shipped 2026-08-19; the remainder is the owner walkthrough only.** `blink()` is gone from `SessionHUDPresenting` and from `RecordingHUDWindow`, replaced by `acknowledgeFlag(count:)` plus `resetFlagCount()`, so the panel-wide `alphaValue` dip cannot come back without re-adding a protocol member. The acknowledgement is now two things on the flag control itself: the design system's own press feedback (scale to 0.97 over `MPMotion.durPress` about the bounds centre, exactly `MPButton.applyPressScale`, with a `signal600` tint releasing to `fgMuted`), and a persistent count badge on the glyph that answers "did that register" after the fact, which is walkthrough step 3's bar. The glyph stays outline and monochrome in every state: the two iconography rules reserve fill for a selected state and forbid decorative tint, and a count is not a selection. **The count cannot disagree with the artifact by construction**, since `flagMoment` passes `markerSeconds.count` and `MarkerFile.write` flushes that same array; `resetFlagCount()` is called at both `markerSeconds` resets so the surface cannot outlive its batch. Press feedback is driven programmatically rather than off `isPressed` because two of the three entry points (hotkey, right-click item) never touch the button, and a `pulseGeneration` guard stops a fast second press being un-tinted by the first one's settle. 12 tests added (4 pure `HUDFlagAcknowledgement`, 3 source fences pinning the removal and the `markerSeconds` derivation, 3 on the real button via `makeContentView`, 2 controller-level); `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2746 tests 0 failures, truth fences clean, no pipeline change. Docs updated in the same commit: `README.md` and `docs/GUIDE.md` both still said the panel blinks. The `design/ui_kits/macos_app/RecordingHUD.jsx` recreation models no icon controls at all, so there was nothing to badge; its header note now says a re-mock owes this state too, which is DSN30's scope rather than this task's. **Owner-owed:** the walkthrough at the end of the spec. Weight `simple` because its step 2 says a manual recording is fine, with no live call and no waiting. Owner approved the move here on 2026-08-19; the spec stays at [`tasks/UX73.md`](./tasks/UX73.md) until the walkthrough closes it. |
