| [DET9](./tasks/DET9.md) | The skipped-meeting latch lapses mid-call, so a dismissed prompt re-opens over the live meeting | Detection | complex | P1 | Shipped 2026-08-05, 2360 daemon tests green (5 new), build green, truth fences clean. The grace is now derived, not hardcoded: `DiscoveryScanCadence.discoveryDefault` names the intervals the watcher actually runs at, and `skipLatchGraceSec = skipLatchGracePolls (5) x idle`, so the original comment's "5x the poll" intent holds by construction and 60 s replaces 15 s. **Of the spec's three shapes, only the grace derivation shipped, and the other two are refuted by measurement rather than skipped.** Reading the owner's `events.jsonl` at pickup: (1) refreshing on any still-a-running-candidate sighting is **actively harmful**, because 293 of 294 `candidate_dropped` sightings of Teams are title-only and they fire around the clock (07:22, 22:54, 23:54, with no meeting anywhere near), so that rule would hold the latch permanently and silently swallow every later Teams meeting, a worse P1 than this one; (2) the cooldown-branch refresh is **dead code at the shipped default**, since latch lapse is `lastSeen + grace` and cooldown expiry is `skip + 60` with `lastSeen >= skip`, so at any grace >= the cooldown that branch is unreachable for a skipped bundle (ARCH6 is an open task about exactly this class). **The spec's own incident reading is corrected in the task file**: the 2026-07-27 re-prompt at 60.5 s is the *cooldown* expiring, not a fresh detection, because a full-signature confident winner (score 11) returned at 48.2 s and the already-lapsed latch let `handleDiscovery` fall through to the cooldown branch. 48.2 s is what sized the grace, with END9's measured 21-33 s control blackout as the second data point. The mic was evaluated as a blackout-immune liveness oracle and rejected: `mic_busy_started` / `mic_busy_ended` have fired **zero** times in the current log generation, so DET3's span tracker produces nothing on this Mac. Five stale prose sites fixed, two more than the spec named (`DetectionStateMachine` grace + `liveSuppressedCandidate`, `MeetingSessionController.handleDiscovery`, `SkippedMeetingLatch`, `StatusBarController` x2, `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s glossary), plus README and GUIDE for the label's user-visible tail. **Owner-owed:** the walkthrough in the task file. `./scripts/rebuild.sh` was deliberately **not** run, since this shipped from an unlanded worktree and installing from there would diverge from `main`; run it after landing, then walk steps 2 to 4. Its load-bearing step is step 3, confirming the latch still lapses when the call genuinely ends: the grace widened 4x, and the accepted cost is that a *different* meeting starting in the same app within 60 s of the last one inherits the latch and is never prompted. |
| [DIAR5](./tasks/DIAR5.md) | The LLM speaker-cleanup pass is a second merge source | Transcription | complex | P1 | **Owner approved the move 2026-08-18, and merged this check with DIAR3's open step 8: one dwell answers both.** DIAR3's step 8 has been open since 2026-07-31 and reads "turn on Clean up speaker labels after transcription and live with it for a few meetings", but this task changed what that toggle does, so as written it now asks for a verdict on behaviour that no longer ships. The walkthrough in [`tasks/DIAR5.md`](./tasks/DIAR5.md) is the merged one and covers both; DIAR3's row below stays where it is and closes on the same run. **All three scope items shipped 2026-08-18, Python only; suite green with 10 new tests (1237 pipeline), ruff + pyright + truth fences clean. Remainder is an owner-run confirmation over real meetings, which is why this row is here rather than in the done record.** (1) **Auditable.** Every applied and refused edit is written per segment to `<stem>.diarize_cleanup.json` (index, from-label, to-label, the model's `kind`, the voiceprint similarity), plus labels before/after/eliminated; written on every run including a no-op one, so presence means the pass ran and absence means it never did. Schema in `CONVENTIONS.md`. (2) **Measured**, and the spec's own before/after turned out to be impossible on existing meetings rather than merely unmeasured: nothing on disk keeps a transcript's pre-cleanup labels, since the pass rewrites `<stem>.json` and `<stem>.md` in place and `<stem>.embeddings.json` holds the finalize label set with no per-segment assignment. Recording `from` is what fixes that going forward, and `scripts/valid1_check.py --cleanup` reads the record back: per meeting, the edits, the refusals, how many of the owner's later corrections landed on segments the pass had relabelled, and the DER with and without the cleanup (reconstructed by `revert_cleanup`). The measurement that *was* possible is stronger than the DER pair anyway: over **36 cleaned meetings** the pass eliminated a label 8 times, and scored against the most acoustically favourable destination available the eight run 0.089, 0.113, 0.289, 0.328, 0.351, 0.463, 0.524, 0.554, so **not one clears the roster's same-person bar of 0.65**; read the other way, 77 label pairs the owner's corrections prove are different people top out at 0.644. (3) **Guard shipped**, keyed off label elimination rather than the model's self-reported `kind`, because that field is unvalidated and both kinds perform the identical mutation, so a guard on it is one the model opts out of by writing the other word. An edit that empties its source label is refused unless the two voiceprints clear `MERGE_MIN_SIMILARITY = 0.65` (`roster.MATCH_MIN`, the project's existing bar), fail-open where a label has no embedding. It refuses all three of the demonstration meeting's collapses. **Named cost, not buried:** the same corpus offers one clean same-person split to measure the other side with (0.531) and this bar refuses that too, so the pass now merges rarely; reattribution is untouched and is the majority of its edits. Default stays **on**, per the spec's own ruling-out of the toggle. **Adjacent item checked and refuted:** the bare numeric keys in the embeddings sidecar are raw diarizer ids for clusters that label no segment in the finalized transcript (`resolve_speaker_labels` maps only ids present in segments; `_write_embeddings_sidecar` falls back to `str(raw_id)`), so they are orphan entries, not mis-keyed ones. Both readers look up by the label the transcript shows (`mp roster enroll --label`, and `VoiceResolution.derive` which uses the keys only as a can-enroll test over transcript-derived cast members), so no wrong-cluster read is reachable and there is nothing for FEAT3 here. Filed 2026-08-04 by DIAR2's closing sweep. `summarization.diarize_cleanup` first ran on real meetings on 2026-08-04 (every earlier event in `pipeline_events.jsonl` is pytest residue), and on the 11-person standup `20260804-103026` it relabelled **25 of 81 segments** (`merges_count=4, reattributions_count=21`), taking the meeting from the 12 labels `<stem>.embeddings.json` records at finalize down to 7; the owner then hand-corrected 21 segments and `--der` scores it 36.6% misattributed with three merged clusters. The structural half is the strong half and does not need more samples: the pass sees `[i] Label: text` and **nothing else**, so with no audio and no embeddings it cannot tell that two labels are different people, while its declared job is to merge labels it judges to be one. That puts it directly against the separation DIAR2's sweep just bought at the clustering layer. P1 because it is on by default, it sits on the exact surface DIAR2 called the broken promise, and its first step is cheap. Scope: make it auditable first (it emits only aggregate counts, so no correction can currently be traced to a cleanup edit), then measure, then consider refusing a merge whose two voiceprints are far apart. Not "turn it off": it did no harm on the other two meetings it touched. **Amended 2026-08-10** with the owner's own independent report of one instance from the UI ("the phrase that should become Atul was named Prajval"), now recorded segment by segment as the demonstration case: in `20260804-103026` segment 59 is Sudip saying "Let's go next with Atul." and segments 60 to 62 are labelled `Prajval`, while `Atul` appears in neither the 12 finalize labels nor the 7 shipped ones. It sharpens the argument rather than repeating it: the pass sees `[i] Label: text` and this input **contained** the handover naming the next speaker, so on this instance it was not blind for want of audio, it held the answer and did not use it. Attribution to the cleanup pass versus the clustering layer is still unprovable from disk, which is exactly why scope item 1 comes first; the instance is a good acceptance fixture either way. |
| [REC12](./tasks/REC12.md) | The prompt's live-waveform tap raises an uncatchable ObjC exception, aborting the daemon within 100 ms of every prompt it hits | Recording | complex | P1 | Shipped 2026-08-17, **every scope item plus the sibling**; items 1, 3 and 4 landed first and item 2, the ObjC `@try` barrier, followed once the owner approved the new target it needs. `GuardedTapInstall` is now the one seam that hands a format to `installTap`: it refuses a zero sample rate or zero channel count, logs `mic_tap_install_skipped` with the calling site, and returns an `Outcome`. Both call sites route through it, `MicLevelMonitor.arm` (the waveform then stays flat, its own documented degradation) and `MeetingRecorder.installMicTap` (`start()` plus device-change recovery, where a refusal ends recovery rather than recording silence). That is the raise condition readable from the format alone, and `AVAudioFormat` really does hold 0 Hz / 0 ch (measured), so a non-nil format was never evidence of a usable one. **That check alone is probabilistic and the barrier is what makes it total:** the remaining path is the format disagreeing with the device between the read and the install, which is a race no pre-check can close (08-13 was AirPods Pro renegotiating to 24000 Hz against 48000 on the 08-14 crashes). **The barrier (item 2):** `MPRunCatchingObjCException` in a new leaf target `MeetingPipeObjC` (one `.m` plus its header, a `Package.swift` entry, no third-party dependency, and deliberately not a Core dependency), because SwiftPM has no mixed ObjC/Swift target; it is the only ObjC in the tree and `GuardedTapInstall` is its only caller, fenced. **A caught raise is terminal for that engine, and the seam enforces it rather than asking callers to**, since the unwind runs through Swift frames whose cleanups never execute and abandons AVFAudio mid-call: the engine is condemned (weakly, by identity), every later tap on it is refused, and `Outcome` distinguishes that from a refused *format*, which stays retryable. Both callers have a live retry path in, which is why a comment would not have held: `MeetingRecorder.start` replaces a condemned engine so one raise costs one recording rather than all of them, and `MicLevelMonitor` just stops metering. Removing the waveform was **not** proposed and was not needed. **The state half is fixed outright:** `MicLevelMonitor` observes `.AVAudioEngineConfigurationChange` on its own engine through `addMainQueueObserver` (then a second hop to its private queue, since its state is not main-confined), and `stop()` no longer gates teardown on `isRunning`, which also closes the residual REC13 documented on `startBounded` (an abandoned start that later succeeds stranded a live tap for the process lifetime). **The next one is self-diagnosing:** `UncaughtExceptionLogger` writes the exception name and `reason` to `daemon.log` before the abort, which is the one string no crash report carries and the reason all four aborts ended with the condition inferred rather than read. **+30 tests across the two passes** (18 first: 7 `GuardedTapInstall` including a source fence over every `installTap` call site, 7 `MicLevelMonitor`, 4 `UncaughtExceptionLogger`; then 12 for the barrier: 6 `ObjCExceptionBarrier`, 5 more `GuardedTapInstall` including a fence that the barrier has exactly one caller, 1 more `MicLevelMonitor`). Every claim was **verified red by measurement**: reverting the format check reddened the zero-sample-rate, zero-channel and configuration-change tests; neutering the barrier to a bare call reddened 8; neutering the condemnation alone reddened 6; the source fence is red against `main` by arithmetic (1 bare call, 0 guarded). **One claim was refuted while measuring:** no test can demonstrate the abort, because XCTest catches ObjC raises itself, so a neutered barrier yields a red test rather than a dead binary; the comments asserting otherwise were corrected, and the abort stays evidenced by the crash reports. `swift build` and `swift build -c release` clean, `swift test` green at 2585, `truth_fences.py both` green, no new event category. The new target adds no `.bundle` and links statically, so `rebuild.sh`'s staging is unchanged (rehearsed on a scratch copy: `codesign --verify` valid), and `install.sh`'s standalone App Intents compile is untouched (re-run: 8 const values). One incidental correction: the level-shaping comment claimed "~0.001 -> ~0.04, ~0.1 -> ~0.7", which the curve has never done; the shaping is unchanged and the numbers now match a test. **Owed:** only the walkthrough in [`tasks/REC12.md`](./tasks/REC12.md), which now starts with a `rebuild.sh` that has not run yet (the barrier landed from a worktree), then a normal working day with no new `.ips`, the AirPods-connecting repro, and a check that the waveform still animates. Original filing: from the owner's crash report ("happens several times already"). **All four MeetingPipe crash reports on this machine are this one bug**, and each lands inside 100 ms of a meeting prompt. `installTapOnBus` raised an `NSException` and nothing caught it, so `objc_terminate` called `abort()`: `EXC_CRASH`/`SIGABRT` on the `MeetingPipe.MicLevelMonitor` queue at `MicLevelMonitor.swift:23`, identical in all four. They chain, since launchd relaunches and the replacement dies at the next prompt it loses the race on (crash 08-13 15:00:25 → relaunch same second → crash 16:24:43; crash 08-14 10:30:38 → relaunch → crash 12:15:38). Correlated against `events.jsonl`, `prompt_shown` precedes each abort by **0.049 to 0.097 s**, with an identical `discovery_shadow_pick` → `starting` → `prompting` → `prompt_shown` → abort shape, then a relaunch at +1.2 s and a re-prompt at +2.6 to +4.9 s. `MicLevelMonitor.start` runs on prompt present, for the prompt's `LiveWaveformView`. Cost so far: on 08-13 15:00 the owner accepted the *second* prompt so the recording starts ~4 s into the call, and the 08-13 16:24 re-prompt produced no recording at all; a prompt that vanishes is a prompt that can be missed against a 30 s skip-default timeout. Blast radius is bounded only because `handleDiscovery` gates on `isAcceptingPrompts`, so no prompt is raised during a recording. Mechanism: `:19` reads `outputFormat(forBus: 0)` and `:23` installs with it, **no validation in between**, at exactly the moment the meeting client is acquiring the device (the 08-13 relaunch resolved AirPods Pro at 24000 Hz, the 08-14 ones the built-in mic at 48000). The existing `do/catch` guards `engine.start()`, which *throws*; `installTap` *raises*, and Swift cannot catch that. The exception reason is not in the report, so the exact raise condition is inferred, not read, and the fix does not depend on it. Scope: validate the format and leave the waveform flat per the file's own stated degradation, add an ObjC `@try` barrier (none exists in the tree), install an uncaught-exception handler so the next one is self-diagnosing, and observe `AVAudioEngineConfigurationChangeNotification` (nothing does, so `isRunning` silently diverges from the engine). `MeetingRecorder.installMicTap:786` is the same unguarded call on the **recording** path, reached from device-change recovery; fence it in the same pass. **P1 as a repeating process abort on the product's core interaction; owner may re-band, since no recording has been killed yet.** Explicitly not the off-main queue (deliberate, the 2026-06-24 stuck-prompt fix), not `boundedEngineStart` (bounds a hang, not a raise), and not deleting the waveform as the first move. Note these are `bug_type: 309` but are **not** the code-signing SIGKILL class; that discriminator is the exception type and an empty backtrace, not the bug type. **Field evidence 2026-08-19, and it answers the spec's one unproven claim.** The next occurrence fired during ordinary use: two `mic_tap_install_raised` rows (the first two in the entire event history) and **zero new crash reports**, so the barrier caught a raise that was previously an abort. The reason string the four .ips files lacked now reads `Failed to create tap due to format mismatch, 1 ch, 24000 Hz, Float32`, which identifies the raise condition as hardware-format disagreement and confirms the Bluetooth hypothesis: the AirPods ran at 24000 Hz through two calls and were back at 48000 Hz when the tap was requested at 24000. Not free, and not a regression against this task's acceptance: the raise now costs a failed auto-start rather than a crash, and the owner recovered the meeting by hand 22 s later. The residual format bug is out of REC12's scope. Details in the spec's `Now proven` section. |
| [REC13](./tasks/REC13.md) | An AVAudioEngine config-change notification deadlocks the engine queue, so a recording never stops and system capture runs unbounded | Recording | complex | P1 | Shipped 2026-08-17; every engineering line of the acceptance is green and the status is the owner-walkthrough clause only. **The deadlock is gone at the root:** the `.AVAudioEngineConfigurationChange` observer registered with `queue: .main`, which makes NotificationCenter wrap the handler in an `NSOperation` and have the **poster** wait on it, and the poster is AVFAudio's own engine serial queue. `addMainQueueObserver` (nil queue plus an explicit `DispatchQueue.main.async`) is the one registration now; its test posts from a background queue and asserts the post returns in under half the handler's duration, and it was **verified to fail against `main`** (post took 0.61 s, the handler's whole hold). **Nothing calls the engine synchronously and unbounded any more:** `EngineTeardown` (removeTap / stop / voice-processing revert, abandon-on-timeout, emitting `engine_teardown_timed_out` with a `path`) covers `stop()` and the termination flush, `recoverCapture` is bounded in its own deliberate stop-then-untap order, and `MicLevelMonitor.startBounded` + `runBoundedSync` cover the last unbounded `engine.start()` (which mattered because that monitor's `start` and `stop` share one private queue, so a wedged start made it un-stoppable with the tap still open). **Second defect, and the finding that relocated the fix:** the termination flush did not merely run slowly on 2026-08-14, it never ran at all. Its first statement is an unbuffered `Log.writeLine` and that line is absent for the whole day, so the block was **upstream** of it in `flushAndExit`; which call blocked is **not established** (the `sample` predates the SIGTERM), so the ceiling is armed there, on a global queue, before `shutdown()`. Quit now force-exits at 9 s whatever is stuck, leaving the intermediates for the orphan sweep instead of needing `kill -9`; `AppDelegateRelaunchTests` fences that 9 s against the flush budgets below it and the ~10 s SIGKILL window above it. **Stuck stops are visible:** `StopStallWatchdog` emits `stop_exceeded_ceiling` with the elapsed time at 90 s and raises a notification. **Not asserted anywhere in the ship:** why `OperationQueue.main` never drained the operation while main sat idle pumping timers. The fix does not depend on it (a nil queue creates no operation to drain), and both the code and `daemon/docs/gotchas.md` say so explicitly rather than retelling the tidy lock story the sample refutes. **+21 tests** (3 observer, 7 teardown, 4 stall-watchdog, 3 MicLevelMonitor, 3 runBoundedSync, 1 ceiling fence); `swift build` clean, `swift test` green, `truth_fences.py both` green, no new event category. Also corrected REC12's scope item 4, which claimed nothing observes that notification. **Owed:** the owner walkthrough in [`tasks/REC13.md`](./tasks/REC13.md) (reproduce a route/display churn, press stop during it, quit mid-recording), which is the only thing between this and done. **Named, deliberately not fixed:** REC9's no-readable-duration fallback prefers the mic, and it is most likely to fire exactly when the process was killed, where the system leg is no more re-obtainable; it needs its own row. |

The mapping is a pure table (`SpeakerAccuracy.swift`) so Custom is a readout that appears only for a value no position pins, never the selectable no-op the local-model picker still has (UX43). **The blocker clause the spec asked about clears**: the cleanup pass does route through the clamp. `cleanup_transcript` calls `entry.prepare` (overlay, then arm the egress guard on the resolved per-meeting config) before any client exists, and `_select_cleanup_backend` resolves through `config.effective_backend`, the TECH-ARCH1 chokepoint that forces `local` whenever `zero_egress` holds; `test_regulated_mode_forces_local` pins it. Copy says both. **Owner-owed:** the spec's eyeball, per the walkthrough in the task file. **Its step 8 merged into DIAR5's check 2026-08-18, at the owner's direction, and is not dropped.** Step 8 asked the owner to turn the cleanup toggle on and live with it for a few meetings; DIAR5 then changed what that toggle does (it refuses a merge whose voiceprints disagree, and writes a per-segment audit record), so run as written it would grade behaviour that no longer ships. The merged walkthrough is in [`tasks/DIAR5.md`](./tasks/DIAR5.md) and covers both rows against the code that actually ships; steps 1 to 7 of DIAR3 are unaffected and still close on this row's own eyeball. |

the 14B class has OOM-hung this Mac before, which is why the run carries a memory protocol rather than a runner. Five of six candidate mechanisms closed a priori with citations (calendar by CAL1 / ADR 0011; model titles by the 168-of-169 measurement; the workflow itself, retained only as fallback; **non-title AX attributes by this repo's own 2026-07-14 live dump at `MeetingAXHandleBuilder.swift:225`, which records that Teams exposes no `AXValue` and no `AXIdentifier` anywhere in its tree**, so title text is the only channel; fuzzy inference by decision). The probe accordingly contains **no title pattern at all**: it measures containment of the owner-supplied meeting name plus byte identity across instances, both format-agnostic. Bars pre-registered before any capture: **A** name present on >= 80% of sampled meetings (reported over all captures and over series captures separately), **B** a name-bearing title byte-identical across every instance of one series, **C** no 1:1 or ad-hoc call colliding with that stable title (the AI7 over-merge guard); GO builds legs 2 and 3, MARGINAL builds leg 2 plus CAL2 only and holds AI6/AI7, **NO-GO is an acceptable pre-registered outcome that narrows CAL2 rather than substituting another mechanism**. Two things beyond the spec: the 3% has a **second candidate cause the spec treated as one**, timing, since `MeetingTitleResolver.resolve` runs exactly once at prompt time from `MeetingSessionController.appSource(from:)`, so a perfect regex still returns nil if the naming window does not exist yet, and the probe separates the two by recording `label_present_at_first_sample` apart from `label_present`; and the probe **refuses to capture or emit any verdict without an Accessibility grant** (verified: it exits 2 in this harness and writes nothing), because a disclaimed parent reads zero windows and that false zero is indistinguishable from a real NO-GO. **Owner-owed:** run the probe during 3 instances of one named recurring series (same `--label`), plus one 1:1 and one ad-hoc, then `swift daemon/scripts/cal3-teams-title-probe.swift --report` from a Terminal you opened yourself with Accessibility granted to it; paste the verdict into the spike doc, and paste the emitted `window_titles.json` entries into the fixture **regardless of the verdict** (it is wrong today either way, and that is what turns `0/21 captured` non-zero). Legs 2 and 3 stay blocked until that verdict exists. |

| [FEAT14](./tasks/FEAT14.md) | The roster computes who a voice almost was, then discards it | Feature | partial | P3 | Shipped 2026-08-18, owner-promoted out of P3 for this batch. **The `partial` is the owner-verification gate alone; no dev remainder is owed.** `RosterStore.match_detail` returns the two-gate verdict **plus** the near miss behind a decline, `match` is the verdict-only reader over it (so no caller that wants a name can act on a suggestion by accident), and `resolve_speaker_labels` returns `(mapping, near_misses)` keyed by the final `THEM-x` label, which finalize writes into `<stem>.embeddings.json` as `near_misses` (schema 2, written only when there is one). Swift reads it through `MeetingStore.nearMisses`, `VoiceResolution.derive` hangs it on `Voice.suggestedName`, and `VoiceResolutionBar.actions(for:)` offers `Name as <person>` as a second item under `Name this voice...` on an unresolved chip, one click into the same `nameSpeaker` enroll the naming sheet runs, so accepting adds the enrolment sample the stalled loop was never getting. **Both gates are untouched and pinned**: `MATCH_MIN` 0.65 and `MATCH_MARGIN` 0.07 are asserted as values and both decline shapes re-asserted, so the suggestion is bought with a floor of its own (`SUGGEST_MIN` 0.50) rather than by lowering the accept bar. **No similarity crosses into Swift at all**, so no surface can render a cosine as a confidence; the item spells the name and carries "not a certain match" under it instead. Three decisions the spec left open: the margin gate gates the suggestion too (a top candidate the runner-up is level with is a coin flip between two real people, so it suggests neither), a suggestion whose person is no longer enrolled is dropped (which subsumes FEAT11's empty-roster rule rather than special-casing it), and accepting emits `coordinator.roster_suggestion_accepted` so "did surfacing near misses actually grow the corpus" is answerable from the log rather than from guessing at sample counts. Verified: `pytest -q` 1268 passed 1 skipped (13 new), ruff clean, `swift build` clean, `swift test` 2615 tests 0 failures (10 new, 2605 on the base), `truth_fences.py both` clean. Pyright 0 errors, but only as `uv run --extra dev --extra mcp pyright`: the command `pipeline/CLAUDE.md` lists in its Build + test block, `uv run --extra dev pyright`, now reports 3 unresolved `mcp.*` imports in AUTO3's `mcp_serve.py`, because CI's typecheck job installs `mcp>=1.29,<2` and that local command does not. A doc-truth gap in the local command rather than a code defect, and not FEAT14's to fix. **Fixed by `46e9d28` the same day**, which changed that block to `uv run --extra dev --extra mcp pyright` and added a note explaining why the extra is load-bearing; the finding above stands as the dated observation it was (HYG8, 2026-08-19). Docs: README's Transcript-tab paragraph, `docs/GUIDE.md`'s cast-strip recipe, ARCHITECTURE's FEAT11 bullet and both sidecar rows, CONVENTIONS' embeddings + roster sections, the design kit's known-lag note. **The remainder, and the only thing owed, is a read-only probe**: over the existing `<stem>.embeddings.json` corpus plus `roster.json`, recomputing `match_detail` per unnamed label to count how many of the 207 summarized meetings would have offered a suggestion. Filed separately as [`FEAT17`](./tasks/FEAT17.md) rather than built here. It exists because the filed walkthrough could not have passed at all (no meeting on disk carries `near_misses`, since only `mp finalize` writes one) and because the rewritten one, around a Re-transcribe, still passes whether or not a near miss exists, so no owner check can tell "works" from "never fires on this library" while the probe answers exactly that for free and for no owner time. Owner took that route 2026-08-18 over spending a re-transcribe. The row stays here rather than moving to [`owner-verification.md`](owner-verification.md): what was approved is the probe, not an owner-verification weight, and none is recorded. [`tasks/FEAT14.md`](./tasks/FEAT14.md) carries both, the probe as the next step and the in-app check recorded under it. |

| [REC26](./tasks/REC26.md) | Flagged moments are written only at stop, so a crash, a quit or a rebuild mid-meeting recovers the audio and loses every flag | Recording | 2026-11-12 | P2 | Shipped 2026-11-12. `flagMoment` now writes `<stem>.markers.json` atomically on every press (the stop-time write stays as the final flush), so a crash, quit or rebuild relaunch mid-recording keeps the flags through orphan recovery; pinned by a controller test (two presses on disk before stop, same markers after), a real-merge orphan recovery test and a MeetingStore one-row test; CONVENTIONS, `MarkerFile` and `mp.markers` docs updated; `bash scripts/ci-local.sh` green. |
| [REC25](./tasks/REC25.md) | The capture writers queue without bound and count only thrown errors, so a stalled disk grows memory about 2 GB an hour unseen, and an abandoned writer's failures can force-stop the next recording | Recording | 2026-11-12 | P2 | Shipped 2026-11-12, closed on tests. SerialBufferWriter counts pending bytes and a high-water mark; WriterBacklogPolicy on the two existing 1 Hz ticks logs writer_backlog past 30 s of a channel's audio and force-stops past 512 MiB across both as recording_error cause writer_stalled (no free-space read on main); WriteFailureRouter gives each writer its own tracker and drops escalations from a recording that is no longer live as write_failure_stale, closing the generation at the top of stop() as well as bumping it in start(). REC10's shared pad buffer is charged once. 25 new tests, swift test 2888 + Core 415 and ci-local all green. Filed 2026-10-25 from the performance review. `SerialBufferWriter.enqueue` is an unbounded `queue.async` with no pending count and the trackers see only thrown errors, so a disk that hangs (a network or file-provider output dir) piles up buffers at about 576 kB/s (2.07 GB an hour) with the HUD showing live levels; separately, `start()` replaces the recorder-owned `WriteFailureTracker`s on main while a writer abandoned by the previous stop keeps reporting into them, so its failures can force-stop a healthy recording or post a false disk-full notice. Scope: a pending-bytes counter, a warn-then-force-stop backlog policy on the existing 1 Hz liveness ticks (never dropping buffers), and a tracker owned by each writer with a recording generation on escalation. [Spec](./tasks/REC25.md) |

Verified by `bash scripts/ci-local.sh` green (swift test 2872 + 415, 9 new, the wiring fence run red first; pytest 1478). |

| [PERF19](./tasks/PERF19.md) | In-call signal polls never slow down: ScreenCaptureKit is enumerated up to twice a second with no in-flight guard, and the adaptive backoff lasts one poll | Performance | 2026-10-26 | P2 | Shipped 2026-10-26. `ShareableContentSignal` polls at 1 s in both window states (the 2 Hz tier retired against END17's dwell); its SCK bridge is a `RequestGate` over an injected fetch with one request in flight, cleared by the completion (even a late one) and handing the result over under its lock, plus a 30 s give-up on an unanswered request (not in the spec: the gate is process-wide, so a dropped callback would otherwise blind the signal until restart); `shareable_content_unavailable` logs once per outage; a nil probe arms the poll when nothing is armed. `AdaptivePollCadence` keys on what polls find (`slow` after 3 steady polls, `fast` on an unannounced change or an unknown read, `fast` after every arm), with each owner's worst case beside the constant; all three callers pass their poll's observation. 14 new tests; `swift test` 3202 green (after rebasing onto PERF10) and `bash scripts/ci-local.sh` green. [Spec](./tasks/PERF19.md) |
| [PERF17](./tasks/PERF17.md) | Discovery keeps scanning and AX-walking through every prompt and recording although nothing reads the result, and scans queue behind a slow one with no in-flight guard | Performance | 2026-10-26 | P2 | Shipped 2026-10-26. `DiscoveryScanGate` (pure, beside `DiscoveryScanCadence`) keeps one discovery scan in flight with at most one pending follow-up and refuses scans while `isAcceptingPrompts` reads false; `Coordinator` sets that closure from `stateMachine.isAcceptingPrompts` and `onIdleTransition` calls `refreshNow()`. Mic-busy sampling and the poll re-arm keep running outside idle, and `MicInUseTier.SpanLatch` latches any span seen there so it never prompts afterwards. Verified: `bash scripts/ci-local.sh` green, 3159 daemon tests (15 new: gate, span latch, two wiring fences). The post-install `events.jsonl` evidence confirms and does not gate. [Spec](./tasks/PERF17.md) |
| [PERF15](./tasks/PERF15.md) | Engaging, arming and disengaging a meeting runs full AX walks, a ScreenCaptureKit wait and queue syncs on the main thread, so the prompt, Record and Stop stall while the meeting app is slow | Performance | 2026-10-26 | P2 | Shipped 2026-10-26. Discovery's `MeetingAXHandleBuilder.build` runs on `axWalkQueue` and engages back on main only if the state still accepts prompts and no newer build was requested (one in flight per bundle; a repeat engage of an engaged bundle is kept, pinned by a test); a record start runs one build from the moment it commits, feeding MicGate, END11's engage and the Leave arm, with MicGate engaging on empty handles past a measured 1.5 s deadline (`micgate_attached` `via`); `ShareableContentSignal`'s initial evaluate runs on its poll queue and `AXLeaveButtonSignal`'s start/stop/arm are async with a session generation; the prompt title resolves off main, in order; a late `.starting` for the app being recorded is confirmed (`lifecycle_late_start_confirmed`). 10 new tests (one measured red against main); `swift build` debug and release warning-clean in the touched files, `swift test` and `bash scripts/ci-local.sh` green. Field evidence after install confirms, does not gate. [Spec](./tasks/PERF15.md) |
| [PERF10](./tasks/PERF10.md) | The Teams mute-button probe and the process-audio signal still poll on the main thread, so a busy Teams or coreaudiod can freeze the UI for seconds during a call | Performance | 2026-10-26 | P2 | Shipped 2026-10-26, mute-probe half only (the ProcessAudioSignal leg went to CONC9, the record-start builds to PERF15). `AXMuteButtonProbe` confines its state to a private serial queue: the health poll is a `DispatchSourceTimer` there (the main-run-loop `defaultScheduler` is gone), the value/title notification handlers hop on with `async`, and the TECH-MIC6 re-arm walk runs there; `start`/`stop` return at once under a lock-guarded generation, re-checked after each read and walk, so a read in flight at a stop never reaches the next start. `start` no longer throws: a failed subscribe logs `micgate`/`ax_mute_button_arm_failed`. Every element it reads gets a 0.25 s messaging timeout through an injected setter (the button and each resolved element), measured against healthy reads beside the constant, and the three strings come back in one `AXUIElementCopyMultipleAttributeValues` call. Optional legs 4 and 5, the single-poller alternative and the P1 re-band were not taken. 9 new tests; the production poll test measured red against the old probe first (4 of 4 reads on main); `swift test --sanitize=thread --filter AXMuteButtonProbeTests` clean; `bash scripts/ci-local.sh` green (3188 daemon tests, 1 listed skip; 1468 pytest) and `swift build -c release` clean. Post-install `sample` evidence not taken (no Teams call). [Spec](./tasks/PERF10.md) |
| [CONC9](./tasks/CONC9.md) | ProcessAudioSignal, HALSystemMuteProbe and HALVoiceActivityProbe change their state from the main thread and the CoreAudio bus queue with no lock, so a process-list change races the 1 Hz poll | Concurrency | 2026-10-26 | P2 | Shipped 2026-10-26. `ProcessAudioSignal` confines every mutable field to a private serial queue; its production poll is a `DispatchSourceTimer` on it (the main-run-loop `defaultScheduler` is gone); `start`/`stop` return at once, bump a lock-guarded generation and hop `async`, so the start-time resolve no longer runs on main; both bus handlers hop `async` and check the generation; the cross-thread public `lastValue` is removed (no production reader). `HALSystemMuteProbe` and `HALVoiceActivityProbe` get the same confinement with `sync` start/stop (so `start` still throws to `MicGate`) and a queue-confined generation. Threading docs corrected in all three and in the coordinator's `liveCallSignalState` comment. 10 new tests, TSan clean (`swift test --sanitize=thread`, three runs); the old code measured red (evaluate on main, start blocked 3.0 s on the resolver, three TSan races in `evaluate`). `bash scripts/ci-local.sh` green. [Spec](./tasks/CONC9.md) |
